ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u4	The next item is the debate on document XVI, No 3. The report has already been printed and distributed. I ask the rapporteurs, Senators Stefano and Pesco, if they intend to integrate it.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u5	Mr President, Minister, Deputy Minister, ladies and gentlemen, in the face of a limited time, today we are bringing to the attention and evaluation of this House a serious, never ideological work, carried out by the Commissions meeting 5a and 14a, to which we have entrusted from the beginning the objective of keeping together, in the ambitious reasoning to which we are called, also the opposition forces, which I will immediately thank here for the availability and collaboration put in place, at all the stages that have accompanied the inspiration first, with the hearings, and then the construction of a document, which I consider to be full-bodied, but not theoretical. The European Council last July intended to integrate the resources of the European Union's multiannual financial framework with the EUR 750 billion of the Next Generation EU programme. These are resources that will be collected on the markets and that decline, for Italy, in approximately 209 billion euros of availability. We are faced with a very political response from the European Union, which rediscovers its vocation of solidarity and inclusion, at a unique historical moment due to the seriousness of the crisis. We are on an appointment with history, in which we can gather the country around a common horizon, to cultivate together the soil of good practices, to succeed also in overcoming the idea of a fragmented country, divided, crossed by sacks of old and new inconveniences, which certainly do not respond to rankings of priorities, because they all ask to be addressed and resolved. We have a single instrument, the Next Generation EU, whose mechanism for recovery and resilience is the most important programme: the objective is to support Member States' investments and reforms in order to promote a sustainable and sustainable recovery from the environmental, social and economic point of view; to improve the resilience of economies, to reduce economic divergences between Member States. In this respect, the European Commission has provided guidance on drawing up national recovery and resilience plans and on the projects to be submitted for funding, indicating what key principles are green and digital transition, equity, macroeconomic stability, accompanied by a coherent investment and reform package. The Government, in the good proposal sent to the Chambers, has indicated numerous and important objectives that put at the centre the growth, development and cohesion declined in many respects, and that it is intended to cover through six separate missions that will be accompanied by necessary reform initiatives. A detailed framework, therefore, which contains principles, objectives and criteria, which, in agreement with my colleague Mr Pesco, we have divided into two major areas: the innovation section - which will deal with my colleague - and the inclusion section, which I am going to present. Let it be clear: they are two macro sets that dialogue; they are, indeed, connected, irreducibly open and permeable to each other. On the question of inclusion, studies, observations and statistical evidence require that not only the direction of the road be changed, but above all its pace; and I am not just referring to the question of the Mezzogiorno, which is still open. The territorial divide we are experiencing, however, is bound to increase as a result of this crisis, if we do not seize the opportunity to follow up on targeted and timely actions. Svimez's estimates leave room for zero alibi and zero attenuating. If the epidemic of the beginning of the year has spared the South and the islands from the spread of the disease thanks to the lockdown, the economic contagion in those areas has become stronger than in the North of the Country and risks to drag itself into its recessive effects much longer. Therefore, the relevance of a plan capable of looking at the new generations is an appointment for our South not to be missed, because it is historical. The revival of the Mezzogiorno is where the Italian difficulties are most concentrated but also the potential; it is the keystone for any ambition of progress and acceleration towards the future of the whole country. For this reason - and I take advantage of the presence of the Government in the figure of Minister Amendola, but also of Deputy Minister Misiani - it is not enough nor more possible to recover the atavistic gap between North and South of the country through the mere application of the clause of 34 percent, because that measure is designed to prevent the existing gap from growing, which is quite different from finally acting to reduce it. The primary need that emerges is to proceed, decisively to the completion of the large communication networks, with particular regard to the railway one but also to the digital one. High speed throughout the country can no longer be called into question either from a material point of view or from an immaterial point of view. The establishment or completion of efficient and integrated transport infrastructure would increase the size of the potential market for companies in the South and the islands as well. In addition, the PNRR's resources could strengthen the ZES, giving the possibility to activate further investments in infrastructure that are essential to promote industrial location. Clearly - they all hope - coordination of the PNRR projects with those of cohesion policy will be necessary in order to avoid overlaps or unnecessary substitutions. A further element for the recovery of the Mezzogiorno will be measures of fiscal and contributory advantage for a sufficient period of time for the recovery of employment and development with cascade benefits for the entire national territory. In short, infrastructure is really the key issue around which to build a perspective of growth in the South and to make this possible it becomes necessary to reduce the bureaucratic burden and simplify the procedures, putting hand to a revision of the regulatory framework of the Code of Procurement. If we don't do it in the key of Next generation EU, we'll have trouble keeping our time commitments. A constant monitoring of the progress of investments and works will have to be established, as well as measures to strengthen, professionalise and specialise in the human resources of public administrations, especially local authorities, and priority should be given to the aggregation of contracting stations in order to increase their technical competence. In the choice of projects for the National Recovery and Resilience Plan, priority should be given to the development of network infrastructure systems, with particular regard to the completion of those concerning TEN-T networks and multimodal nodes with the adoption of the best technologies to facilitate connections in the South of the country, as well as between the continental part and the islands, in order to ensure effective territorial continuity with the natural intermodal platforms of the coastal arches of the Mediterranean. The criterion of the strategic value of Europe must also be pursued in view of the benefits and real development that it will be able to guarantee in the areas in which it will insist. Within this mission it has emerged the need to follow up investments also on the ramifications of the great arteries, to favor the full involvement of all the productive areas and to tourist vocation of the Country, thanks also to the creation of possible logistic junctions and allowing the true development of sustainable mobility. This set of measures is therefore aimed at filling and reducing also those new gaps that are feeding in other parts of the country. With regard to urban mobility, we also saw the need to support smart city projects, intelligent zero impact mobility interventions. At this point I would also like to stress the need to include airport investments as a priority objective, considering that these infrastructures and airports are vital for the economic, commercial and tourist activities of the country, also responding to other missions of the guidelines. With regard to the equality and gender mission, we have done an excellent job and here it is necessary to make an important premise. In the report we stated the principle that gender policies are a priority and will have to characterize all the missions indicated in the guidelines and all the projects presented in the framework of the recovery fund, so much so that we wanted to establish the need for ex ante evaluations with respect to gender impact in each of the six missions identified. Work, welfare and education are the three directions identified, strongly interconnected, on which to build the change necessary to get out of this crisis. Therefore widespread network of services to the family, such as kindergartens and insertion of care works among the activities to recognize social and economic value. It is also crucial to modernise the economic system by encouraging the creation of jobs and adequate forms of income protection by investing in the training of workers, but also in the unemployed. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Finish it, Senator. STEFANO, rapporteur. The strengthening of active labour policies and the fight against undeclared work, as well as the protection of health and safety at work, are equally crucial, also in order to preserve the principle of genuine competition between businesses. Give me two minutes, Mr. President. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. I've already gone further, Senator Stefano, so you need to tighten and close, please. STEFANO, rapporteur. It is therefore called for the NRP to be integrated with an extraordinary programme for women's employment, closely linked to measures to eliminate pay differences, increase the qualification of women's work and guarantee decent working conditions: these are interventions that are no longer intended as gender policies, but as genuine and authentic public welfare policies. It is also necessary to pay attention to the protection and social inclusion of vulnerable people who are not self-sufficient, such as the elderly and the disabled, and to caregivers who care for them. Finally, the PNR should address new measures and resources to finance the reconstruction of seismic craters in 2009 and 2016. In conclusion, last but not least, health comes: the last few months have demonstrated the centrality of the sector, not only in response to the epidemiological emergency, but also as a fundamental garrison for assistance and social support in the territories. The main challenges of the National Health Service are the inequalities in services on the territory: a useful tool to recover this disparity is to pursue the model of proximity health, relying on the potential of digital, which allows to increase the effectiveness and efficiency of services also on integrated home care. There is also a need to address the issue of obsolescence of the instrumentation provided in many health care facilities. It is also necessary to pay attention to the complex forms of youth distress, investing even more on the counter-dependence programmes all. These, Mr President, are a small part of the demands made for the sector: I know that this is not the time, nor did we want to force this issue into the report, because it was not the right opportunity; but I believe that this House is the right place to start a sincere confrontation, free from conditioning, on the opportunity of accessing the Health MES, freed as it is from the so-called stigma of heavy conditionality and the spectre of the Troika. We've been asked by almost every audition. We are in the most serious crisis since World War II. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Senator Stefano, please close. STEFANO, rapporteur. We are in a context of great uncertainty. We are called to build a future that will make us look forward and not go back: we therefore have the opportunity of a new chapter to be written with the strength of our choices, a policy based on courage and hope. But this is the moment of choices and it is time to overcome the fences. I conclude by saying that this plan, with its importance, does not allow us to be considered an issue that is not only up to the Commission to manage, but rather as a more tiring, demanding, but absolutely necessary teamwork run by all the different declinations of political representation. Yesterday we decided and wanted to write clearly that the Chambers will have to be an active part in the identification of the projects and we asked the Government to report periodically on the progress of the procedures. For this reason it is important that Parliament - and I have really finished - is fully and in advance involved in the dialogue between the European Commission and the Government, regarding the activities of selection and definition of the projects of the PNR, therefore starting from the draft of the plan. We are calling for this, before being sent to the Commission, to be sent here to Parliament for its due approval. PRESIDENT. - The debate is I kindly ask both the rapporteurs and the senators to stick to their time.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u6	Mr President, allow me to share the joy I have in my heart for having succeeded, together with President Stefano, in bringing to the House a shared relationship, not with all the groups, but with all the senators I have to say. All the senators concerned had the opportunity to say their own and to include in this opinion what you need to do through the resources of the Next generation EU for a better country [[Applause]] We know that the three pillars on which Next generation EU is based are the environment, modernisation and social inclusion. Let us start with the environment, the ecological transition, the green transition, which must refer to any project we are going to finance in the near future. This is because we must not treat the environment as a separate theme, but as a theme that must be permeated with any action that we will take from here to the future [[Applause]] If we don't put this in our head, we're not going anywhere. We have one Earth, one planet, we do not have one escort, so we must act quickly on this, as the President of the European Commission Ursula von der Leyen reminded us. Our Government, together with eight other countries, has pushed for Next generation Eu to take place, this is our chance and we cannot miss it. Ecological transition is intended first of all to repair the damage that has been caused to the environment over the years and therefore to be able to intervene where there are the most critical: I am talking about air, soil, water. Let's start with air pollution: many Italian areas are really affected by the problem of pollution, so we must act quickly with precise solutions and effective instruments against pollution, logically starting from the three sectors where air pollution is created: mobility; industry and homes, therefore civil sector. We need sustainable mobility once and for all, so not only abandoning fossil fuel, but also looking at, going further, thinking about electric mobility, public mobility, sharing mobility, abandoning the concept of self-sustaining car to have shared means and tools to move us all more green, more environmentally friendly, and above all creating systems that become intermodal, so that any individual has the possibility to opt between a ceiling of sustainable choices. In addition to mobility, the industry sector needs to be considered. Industrial processes need to be retrained; we have companies that unfortunately still use archaic processes that are very impacting on the environment; now we have the possibility to develop projects aimed at converting our companies. I would like to talk about the production process, what to produce, but I will do it later. We must also tackle the housing sector, the civil sector. Well, we have already done a lot in this field through the ecobonus, now we have the superbonus. Through the resources of the Next generation EU we will have the opportunity to refinance these tools so that truly our public and private real estate assets can know a collection and become really energy efficient. On air and soil we must also intervene in areas of complex crisis, allowing me to use this term also from an environmental point of view. We have the sites of national interest (SIN) and the sites of regional interest (SIR); we all have to be completely retrained, we have directives to apply; we intervene, we do the reclamations, because the environment asks us and, as I said before, we only have one, so we have to act quickly and we have to do it, because we also have the problem of water on the ground. We need to think about the water, the purifications. To this end, we have a Commissioner ad acta and now, through the funds of the Next Generation EU, we must give the necessary resources for the Commissioner to restore a controlled water regime that limits the impact on the environment of polluted waters. As for the purifiers, which in Italy however there are, new ones must be made, but above all the existing ones must be converted, in the sense that they must be made to work well. It is a very simple thing, but at present it is not so; moreover, if a purifier does not work well, it pollutes more than if it were not there. We must therefore act quickly through the construction of new purifiers and the upgrading of existing ones; but above all, we need people, skills, because the purification of water needs specific and specialized skills and unfortunately we have few in Italy. So we really need to intervene on this through projects: Next generation EU is made up of projects, these are not things that start to start, but we need to create projects for public administrations to have the necessary tools to start and then assign the necessary tools to continue these policies. There are many directives on water and the protection of marine territory to be complied with: the Habitat Directive, the Water Framework Directive, the Flood Directive. These are measures that we should have had to implement for a long time: we have not done so yet and we must do so now. We must also talk about eco-compatibility and the protection of biodiversity. Unfortunately the biodiversity of seas, lakes, rivers, humid environments, swamps is in crisis. We must not only implement the directives, but also create the right instruments and projects that protect biodiversity. [[Applause]] The environment is a very important area. We now have an opportunity to intervene and we have to do so. We can't not talk about energy. In the Commission, Senators Buccarella, Errani and President Girotto have reminded us that we must start a transition through the redevelopment, renewal and strengthening of new energy policies, such as hydrogen: many countries, such as France and Germany, have invested billions on this and we cannot stand back. We also need to invest in research to find the best system for the production, transport, use and storage of hydrogen; we are absolutely focused on this. Let us not forget the industrial sector: the conversion of production processes is indispensable; above all, in order to limit the impact of what is produced, we must think from the beginning of what we produce in order to achieve it in a green way. For example, I can't help but talk about the zero-waste cycle: we have to start from the beginning in designing a product that is compatible with the environment throughout its life cycle. [[Applause]] These are things that we have been repeating for years and perhaps this is the very good time to be able to intervene. How can we not talk about the impact of cities? Let us think of urban regeneration, which, in this case too, must be environmentally friendly. Finally, we can talk about zeroing soil consumption, through three very simple systems: we must absolutely try to avoid the transformation of agricultural areas and natural areas into built areas; we must try to limit the damage created by waterproofed areas; above all, we must do everything possible to compensate for the construction of indispensable structures and buildings, with compensatory activities where we have to build on natural areas. All this can be done if culture changes in Italy and, in order to change culture, we have to invest in school, education, research, university, sectors unfortunately left behind. [[Applause]] How do we do that? Let's start with the wrappers, the schools. We need to get rid of the chicken classes and we need more schools. We need to reset the existing ones, make them anti-seismic and fire-fighting, but above all create new schools, because they serve classes with fewer pupils. Teachers must have the opportunity to teach in an easy and effective way and, in the same way, pupils must be in a position to learn easily. This requires more classrooms, more schools, but above all different learning and teaching cycles: it is therefore necessary to invest in teacher training. We cannot forget the people who have the most difficulties in the school environment and who therefore need support: we invest a lot on the training and the number of support teachers, to really think about who needs the most. We cannot talk about school unless we talk about research: we must invest in research, make sure that Italian researchers who are in Italy have the opportunity to express their skills in the best way. Above all, we need to do everything possible to bring our researchers back to Italy, through systems and research paths that are also innovative: we need to create research paths tailored to the researcher's needs, so that there really is the possibility of working and doing research in an easy way, in a properly funded way, otherwise we will remain on the stake also on this front. In connection with this subject, we certainly cannot help but think of industries and the private sector, which need a lot of research. In this case Italy has been particularly penalized over time, because companies are always very small. We try to copy what they have done abroad: small companies must be helped to join forces to create a single research sector, perhaps shared, in order to achieve better results. Mr President, in connection with what Mr Stefano said about inequality, the financial crisis and the pandemic have put us in the face of an ever-increasing problem: the increase in the social class (even if the term is incorrect) of the impoverished, those who have lost their jobs or who will lose it shortly. There are certainly social shock absorbers, but it is not enough; we must do everything we can to support these people and not derail them in that furrow called usury. Let us do everything possible to ensure that there are adequate instruments, such as debt and over-indebtedness crisis management, to ensure that these people can live happily ever after. [[Applause]] Mr. President, I'm gonna stop here. I once again express the joy I have in my heart for sharing this relationship with you. We do everything we can to change our country, because united we win and divided we lose. And we can't lose. [[Applause.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u7	I would like to inform the House that any acts of direction will have to be submitted before the end of the debate.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u8	Mr President, fellow Senators, Government, the report we are about to vote on envisages the dual aim of reducing the gap between the different parts of the country and, at the same time, ensuring that these can develop and modernise with a recovery of the previous gap between North and South, which is still previous even on a post-unitarian historical basis, realising an investment for the future. We would like it to be clear that all the measures identified are suffering from the North-South gap, but that the specific indication of the Commission of the European Union does not end with the re-launching point of the Mezzogiorno, but with all the indications contained in the report on time. There are some sectors, however, such as mobility infrastructure, referred to in mission number 3, in which investment needs in some areas of the Mezzogiorno are well above the clause of 34%, which does not appear sufficient to operate the expected rebalancing, being this a minimum measure of caution aimed at establishing a criterion of planning investments in proportion to the resident population and, therefore, not sufficient and adequate to promote the reduction of territorial gaps still existing today between the different areas of our country. To this end, we reiterate the primary need to proceed decisively to the completion of the large communication networks, with particular regard to the railway and the digital one, allowing the realization of the participations of the national network in optical fiber and the development of the 5G networks with overcoming the digital divide, not to mention that, with the realization or the completion of efficient and integrated transport infrastructures, reducing the costs of the transport connected to the distance, the dimension of the potential market for the enterprises localized in the Mezzogiorno would widen. The resources of the PNRR, moreover, could strengthen the special economic zones (ZES) allowing to activate the investments necessary for the realization not only of the infrastructures previewed in the respective strategic plans, but also of others indispensable ones in order to favor the industrial localization, like those in the retroporti, or to assure the intermodalità of the nets of the marine, rail and road transport. It is important that the document says that the selection of infrastructure projects should give priority to the adoption of the best existing technologies in order to promote stable links in the South of the country, as well as between the mainland and the island, in order to ensure an effective regime of territorial continuity with territories that represent the natural intermodal platform of the coastal arches of the Mediterranean, and this formulation leaves room for intervention; but it is not enough without the identification of a specific vision with references to clear projects in which the dimension of the stable crossing of connected infrastructures is perfectly coherent in terms of real social progress, territorial cohesion and the reduction of the gap between North and South. I am referring specifically to the only strategic infrastructure for the Mezzogiorno: the bridge over the Strait of Messina, which does not mean neglecting all the other infrastructure necessary for the South, but, on the contrary, through careful and targeted planning and implementation of the already planned compensation works, means giving the start to all the infrastructures made usable precisely by the stable crossing of the Strait. Therefore, those who still disapprove of the importance of this work as a whole, insisting on the priority of road and rail infrastructure, must understand that not only one work does not exclude the other, but that the bridge is directly functional to all the works of the Mezzogiorno as a fundamental pillar in support of an effective implementation of trans-European networks - transport (TEN-T) in the logic expressed by the European Parliament. Really guarantee the high speed, for example from Turin to Palermo, without interruptions and all the degrees of the urban nodes through a new concept of station, considering the role that the railway connection can determine with strong economic and productive impact and with the activation of ZES, with the ports, the interporti and the connections from and to the Asian and African Countries that will dominate the economic scene in the near future and to which already the great world markets are looking, indicates an opening to the world with a vision far-sighted in terms of geopolitics, perhaps still difficult to understand considered the retrosia dictated from an ideological short-sightedness that distances a future in which from Sicily would open the door to Europe, enriching first Italy. As has been said, therefore, with the Rome-Pescara that we consider indispensable the work of the territory as a symbolic project, the node on the Strait is fundamental as a priority work as an infrastructure system of Mediterranean scale with global and local effect, in order to finally give a courageous response to the South in an overall vision of Country. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u9	Senator Collina is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u10	Mr President, I want to use these few minutes to first make some overall reflections, of which the Government is certainly aware, and then an evaluation on a specific subject. What Next generation EU puts before us is a commitment articulated in the definition of both a vision that expresses itself with objectives measured on the Italian reality, both of the actions that as a whole lead to achieving those objectives, and of that part of actions that are among those that can be financed by the European Union. On the vision I want to underline the concept of resilience that expresses, as well as an attitude, in this case also a condition of this program. It means that, after this investment season, the country will have to be structured in such a way that, if a great global crisis should happen again, the ability to adapt, namely resilience, will have to be enough to face it, because resources for massive investments like these will not be again in the future. The European Union is also asking us to integrate the extraordinary resources with the resources already normally allocated in the seventies, and I believe that this is a very important part of the work that needs to be vigorously developed. Who in our country has experience, structures, skills grown over the years are the Regions that have always planned, invest and report millions of euro invested for the modernization of the territories, that is the ordinary European resources, those that - as I said before - must be integrated with consistency with respect to the objectives of the Next generation EU. The Regions are also decisive in order to orient the grounding of the extraordinary resources that, even if they are part of infrastructural actions and transversal to the national territory, must also be decisive for the reduction of the scissors, to fill the distances and ultimately to make our Country more homogeneous. This leads to the understanding that the objectives will be achieved with investments that see the regions as protagonists, for knowledge and skills matured on the funds of the European Union, but also with reforms that will have to make the country more competitive. I do not hide a certain concern in seeing this confrontation and the joint work with the Regions that I consider decisive; a work and an institutional but also political confrontation, given the different leadership of the Regions, but, being centered on concrete and decisive issues, they lend themselves to being free from the instrumentality that I see abounding, also associated with a certain impalpability of proposal, in the classrooms of Parliament. Here I note incidentally that Parliament will necessarily have to devote itself to completing the institutional reforms that give our democracy contemporary. The first step was taken a few weeks ago with the confirmed referendum on the cut in the number of Members. Now there is a second decisive step to be taken. Thinking of modernising Italy with investments and economic reforms and leaving the institutions behind means forgetting the origin of the order. I'm closing the parenthesis. In January, a task force of 40 European experts will judge our projects, those that we will apply for financing, but they will also have to see the rest, that is, what must be put on the field to support and give chance of success to the transformation of the country to which those projects must contribute. From this point of view, the Democratic Party, when an innovation arrives, I believe must commit itself to being on the side of change and not on the side of the defense of the existing, with battles of rearguard. This is the soul of reformism. But if it is reformism that governs and is not willful, then it proposes with determination the possible transitions, that hold together change and solidarity. In other words, they are transitions that can build the community of the future. I conclude with a specific assessment of the tourism sector. The question to answer is how to help parts of our tourist system, how to redefine also some roles to return a more competitive system on the international market in the new context that has been created. I realize that it is necessary, above all today, to support the current reality, but now we must put in place a design that, moreover, has never had the resources available to become a reality. Usually, the lack of resources has also prevented the graduality of the transformations and has made the defense of the existing triumph. It will be weeks of decisive work, but with this awareness, with Government and Regions, we do our best. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u14	Mr President, I shall use the minutes at my disposal to draw the House's attention to something that we particularly care about. I thank the rapporteurs for this measure, the Senators Pesco and Stefano, for having accepted this very important aspect, which is also being discussed at European and international level and which has finally entered into the debate in Italy. I am referring to the impact of climate change on our artistic and landscape heritage. A study by Legambiente together with the Ministry of the Environment, entitled "The Italian cities to the challenge of climate," 2017 reminds us that in Italy 28,483 sites of our heritage are exposed to floods, with times of return, and therefore recovery, up to two hundred years. More than 39,000 are, on the other hand, cultural assets exposed to phenomena of rare but extreme intensity, with a return up to five hundred years. There are 200,000 architectural, monumental and archaeological assets, 3,400 museums and about 2,000 archaeological sites, such as Sibari, exposed to floods. I quote Sibari because it is more recent, the flood of which it has been subject. Then we risk landslides. And then there are our cities of art: in Rome are at risk of flooding 2,204 real estate of historical value, with a time of return up to five hundred years (among these the Pantheon and Piazza Navona). We have Florence: 1,145 are the sites, with two hundred years of return time (including the basilica of Santa Croce and Santa Maria del Fiore). Then we have all our villages, including Volterra, the cliff of San Leo in Rimini, Civita di Bagnoregio. And then we have Venice and Matera, our beautiful Matera, the city on the rocks. We also have all the risks of forest fires and droughts. So let's talk about all our landscape heritage, and let's remember that, when we talk about artistic heritage, we cannot avoid talking about the landscape heritage in which all our beauty is set and without which it would not have value. Many documents have been drawn up. One - for example - is recently updated: the Policy Document on the Impact of Climate Change on UNESCO World Heritage Sites, of which I quote one of the recommendations, which is to implement global operational climate mitigation environments that lead to a 50% reduction in carbon footprint in the management of heritage sites compared to the values of the reference year 2020. We have drawn up the National Climate Change Adaptation Strategy, which reminds us that we must do an ordinary and extraordinary maintenance work in the direction of protecting and preserving our heritage. But we must also take care of training in this field, because our cultural heritage is not just something to protect. As the Climate heritage network's Action Plan, launched on the occasion of the Cop25 in Madrid reminds us, our cultural heritage is also a valuable reservoir of know-how to which we can draw to support the fight and the fight against climate change. So, it's not just something to protect, it's also an active resource that can help us in this battle. And it reminds us of a fundamental text, which was presented in 2019 in Baku, edited by Icomos, entitled "Future of our pasts: engaging cultural heritage in climate action" (we commit cultural heritage to the battle against climate change). [[Applause]] So, thank you for showing sensitivity. I also hope that, in practice, there will be powerful investment in maintenance and training; I hope that there will be integrated policies at European, national, regional and municipal level. Another important aspect, that goes in the direction of an implementation of the job offer, and therefore of a strengthening of our labour market, is the protection of the professions that operate in the field of cultural goods, with a necessary recognition also of their status as professionals, and of all those who work in the live show in artistic activities. Mr President, the pandemic emergency has lifted the carpet and put us in front of the large amount of dust accumulated. Today we must take note - as a document from the Art and Show Forum reminds us - that we still do not have a statute for performing and arts workers. [[Applause]] , a statute for atypical workers, for which we must provide for an ad hoc welfare pension system. Only by protecting these professions and providing for an ad hoc welfare system and a facilitated tax system; only by establishing measures to promote their mobility in the European and international context and by providing for the redefinition of the criteria for the distribution of the Single Fund for entertainment, will we allow this country to share also from culture, as I have rightly read in the policy lines of the National Plan for Resilience and Relaunch. Otherwise, we risk having a restart in a dystopic context of cities where the impoverishment of cultural offering will be accompanied, playfully and inevitably, by our spiritual and intellectual impoverishment [[Applause E]] We cannot afford this, first of all because economic restart must take into account a great renaissance of our humanity: it is in fact only with a great renaissance of our humanity that we can truly live a new era. ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u16	Mr President, I would first like to express my satisfaction with the content of this document, namely the report by the Commissions meeting 5a and 14a on guidelines for the definition of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan. On page 25, on environmental issues, there is an express reference to the need to implement the use of the current findings of hydrogen green, aligning Italy to European countries such as France and Germany, with the aim of freeing us in the phase of energy transition from a highly climatic hydrocarbon such as gas. This passage, which I believe is also taken up in the opinion of the Environment Commission, is very important. Speaking of hydrogen - a subject that, as you know, I am particularly passionate about - as a technological revolution in progress, which will change the very economic, financial, industrial and mobility aspects of our societies in a handful of years, it is essential to keep the focus on something that needs to be taken into account. We are talking about hydrogen green, that is, hydrogen produced by the use of renewable sources to have energy and to separate the molecule of hydrogen from water, with instruments called electrolytes. On this point, I would like to point out that the European Union's new hydrogen strategy provides for the production of 6,000 megawatts from electrolysers by 2024. This means that 1,000 megawatts by 2024 will have to be realized in Italy only for the electrolysers, while double in terms of renewables for the energy needed: we must therefore increase the energy from renewable sources, but we must also invest and strongly stimulate the electrolysers, which allow the closure of the virtuous circle of the production of energy and its use in all areas. We know that hydrogen impacts more than the six missions indicated in the recovery fund guidelines: it impacts not only on green revolution and ecological transition, for the obvious reason of the contrast to climate change achieved through a cycle that does not include any type of climate emission, but also, from the intelligent perspective of a forward-looking policy, on the innovation of the productive system and mobility infrastructure (each of these is one of the six missions), as well as on research and development. We must give energy and strength, encourage our universities and our polytechnics so that we can also find technical solutions - perhaps Italian patents, why not - so that our national companies are at the forefront of the production of these technologies. It is essential to stress the importance of green hydrogen because it is opposed in the debate that is taking place today among those involved in the so-called blue hydrogen, that is, the one obtained through methane, with an industrial process that - ahinoi - involves the production of carbon, carbon dioxide. One element of concern on my part is that last month the Government promised to invest a considerable amount - we were talking about 1.6 billion - in a mega CO2 storage project at 2,500 meters underground similar to those in progress in the North Sea. Let's talk about the areas near Ravenna. The opposition to this project, which whoever has understood the importance of hydrogen green wishes not to go into port, is motivated by the fact that it clearly contrasts with the virtuous European indications and the scientific community on the need for processes that do not produce carbon dioxide; by the fact that it subtracts resources from virtuous processes for the creation of green hydrogen and, finally, from the fact that it involves risks. Think only of events of a seismic nature or other, which could endanger the populations and territories in which those great deposits are located, which in the past have already been financed in a similar way by the European Union, but none of them has been realized. It is therefore a losing technology, not virtuous and even dangerous. And, therefore, my input is to follow up what is written today in the document before the Senate, about the need to encourage only green hydrogen and instead to discourage any other form of hydrogen production that is not environmentally sustainable and economically to be encouraged. Finally, I should like to conclude by saying that perhaps we should remember - as I have had the opportunity to do before the Commissions meeting, and I would like to thank President Pesco in this regard, for having also referred to my speech, in addition to those of Senator Errani and other colleagues who have spoken on this point - that hydrogen is part of the six strategic and programmatic chains of the so-called Important project of common European interest (IPCEI). The money we can spend best is the money for hydrogen technology. Colleagues, today you read that if we talk about the field of mobility, hydrogen is not comparable, economically, with other products, such as oil, diesel or petrol. If this is true today - as all experts say - it will not be true in five or six years' time. Being in time, with technologies, preparation, a distribution network, research and the creation of expertise in every area, is an element that will make Italy a modern country and in line with the objectives of the survival of the planet. That is my wish and our wish. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u25	Mr President, senators and senators, representatives of the government, the Covid-19 pandemic has highlighted the close link between human health and the ecosystem, so the imbalance we have created with this economic model between the economic, ecological and social systems. The urgency has therefore emerged to intervene to protect the biocapacity of our common home and to restore the planetary borders now exceeded. When I talk to you about planetary boundaries, I intend to refer to the new sustainability indicators recognised worldwide by the Stockholm Resilience Centre and Italy has exceeded four of these boundaries: climate change, soil consumption, biodiversity and the imbalance between nitrogen and phosphorus cycles. Today I am only going to focus on a part of the guidelines that we are discussing, the one that concerns the ecological transition. We have already heard about the various reforms and investments in energy, transport, decarbonisation, industry, the circular economy, ecodesign and prudent waste management, management and protection of the water cycle, biodiversity. We have focused on reducing greenhouse gas emissions through the increasing use of renewable energy, hydrogen green, energy efficiency of buildings and sustainable mobility. We are talking about 78 billion measures that can contribute quickly to the transition between a brown and linear economic model to a green and circular model and each individual investment must lead us to climate neutrality and to the achievement of the 17 Sustainable Development Goals of the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. So you understand how it is necessary that economic resources are not used in ordinary projects, perhaps creating a doubling of existing resources. We need to focus on projects that accelerate change and that act immediately positively on the quality of life of citizens, on our businesses, on production lines, transport and energy storage. One important aspect that has not been highlighted so far concerns the way in which these projects are chosen. There must be new methods of evaluation and - I repeat - we will have no improvement if we do not use other environmental and social indicators, alongside economic indicators such as gross domestic product, which are good for the market. A systemic approach and comprehensive accounting should therefore be used. This means counting, yes, money, as we have always done well in recent years, but now matter and energy are important, because we do not have plenty of them. For this reason, the 13th Commission has included in the text a part on the need for a European Union-wide dialogue, in order to ensure that the evaluation of projects does not have as the sole criterion the gross domestic product, but also the measurement of the effects on employment, on the social and environmental impact and can therefore be included indicators such as fair and sustainable well-being, because it is able to measure the true well-being of citizens. [[Applause]] and calculation tools such as input-output analysis, physical units, material footprint, which few people know but is the indicator that truly enables decoupling between sustainable development and growth and the use of natural capital. So no more waste of natural resources, natural capital, no more waste of energy, no more inequality. This must be done with the plan under discussion. It is not about making money capital, but about protecting the network of life we have on our planet, the fight against climate change, the resolution of the economic damage created by Covid-19. The development of a fair and inclusive economic and ecological model are sides of the same coin. The challenge of inequality and poverty travels on the same track as the fight against environmental degradation: everything is connected. We have global problems, which is why we have talked about green new deal, which is a green pact, which must be tightened worldwide. I participated in Cop 25 and Cop 24 and in the relevant treaties and I was always a little sorry. Indeed, if we do not have a clear and serious pact at world level and if the treaties do not go ahead, Italy can do its utmost but it will not be enough. The green new deal unites all nations, stakeholders, experts, businesses, public administrations, citizens. I would like to conclude my speech by thanking personally and on behalf of the five stars of Movimento - which today, in the words of President Pesco, have shone - the Government, President Stefano and all those present in the Assembly, who have contributed to the drafting of a truly innovative and meaningful text for the future of our country and for the future of our habitat, our environment. We are no longer at the moment when we need to unbundle. Perhaps it has not been understood that we have come to have very serious problems, that there are people who suffer and who die. We will have a second wave of Covid-19 and I do not understand how we can still, in a Senate Chamber, give speeches sometimes by dockers. Excuse me for saying this, but that's how it comes to mind. Three broadly consistent sources: EUR 30 billion per year for a total of EUR 90 billion. Well, the recovery fund and other European funds related to recovery must not be a mere doubling of ordinary funds, but, guided by a conceptual leap, they must be that "turbo" capable of radically transforming our country, making it stable in a sustainable and qualitative growth to make us compete with the best world systems in guaranteeing social peace, well-being, justice and wealth. However, it is not only the evidence of the disasters and the incalculable environmental, social and economic costs that must commit us; it is also the fascination that the green transition assumes through a wise use of technology and technology, of the basic research of applied research. Let's think about the new scenarios that are opening up in urban regeneration for cities, in the search for new materials, in two-dimensional materials, that can lead us to the zero waste target. Let us think of biofuels, bioplastics of non-carbon derivation; let us think of the extraordinary opportunities that are opening up with energy policies based on hydrogen; let us think of green mobility, mechanical recycling, plant policy linked to waste, the clinical recycling of plastics, capable of breaking them down into primitive molecules. We also think of the possibility of finally producing 100% recycled plastic bottles in our country, a goal just achieved in the August decree, thanks to the will of the majority, but also the support - it must be acknowledged - of all the oppositions in this House. Finally, a final thought, Mr President. The great steps forward of the different civilizations were made when the technique in the different declinations was at the service of a great vision, of a great idea of collective progress of all and of each. Just as the Marshall Plan had among its objectives that of building the European Union after decades of unspeakable devastation, so this new season must be for us the beginning of a new idea of Europe, which makes it united, strong, competitive and decisive both in the internal scenario, and in the global geopolitics. Only in a strong vision of this type could we speak for us and for our children of recovery and residence plan: recovery and resilience, not as a missed opportunity, but as a new beginning. ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u45	He's enlisted to speak Senator Mautone. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u46	Mr President, Government, ladies and gentlemen, the spirit behind and pervading the entire programme of the guidelines of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan is already in its definition. The recovery presupposes a restart and look with confidence to the future; resilience is the ability to face and overcome a traumatic event or a period of difficulty, logically a period like what we have lived and are living, but it is also the ability to adapt to the disadvantageous situations. The plan runs through six clusters or missions, each consisting of a set of homogeneous and functional projects. In particular, as far as the health sector is concerned, the plan is not just an emergency plan, but will have to be a reform project, capable of looking beyond the pandemic, despite the complexity of the moment, an investment plan for health that looks ahead for the next decade. This policy change can and must also be a cultural change. Health protection is not simply a cost or a number, but something living, to be cultivated, to be defended, a fundamental investment for the respect of the individual and the community. The whole health system needs to be reorganised and strengthened, recognising the fundamental importance of the interrelationship between social, environmental and health policies. This programme provides for a substantial revision of the models of governance and organisation, a substantial reorganization of territorial medicine and the primary care network, increasingly integrating general medicine into the National Health Service, trying to reduce and smooth one of the critical points of our health care, the inequalities - in some cases significant - of the services offered, both qualitatively and quantitatively, in the different regions. The plan must offer solutions to guarantee all Italian citizens the same health rights. Professionalism and skills: this is the turning point that must guide the biggest economic operation of the post-war period. We need the utmost attention and surveillance. Improvisation and incompetence in fund management can produce deleterious effects, almost the risk of mafia or other forms of organised lawlessness being infiltrated. One point to stress, present in the plan, is the interconnection of the different missions. For example, health is to be considered transversal to others. I would like to draw the attention of this House to a number of comments which, in my opinion, confirm and reaffirm this concept: in mission 1, the digitisation of the production system, a priority is the digitisation of the health system, in order to improve the services and home care and to better plan the needs of citizens. In mission 2, dedicated to the green revolution, we expect the mapping and reclamation of toxic discharges, such as the land of fires, which we sadly know well, and the safety of health facilities. In mission 3, focused on infrastructure and mobility, infrastructure should be provided to quickly reach and access health facilities, so that they are no longer cathedrals in the desert. In mission 4 on education, training, research and culture, ample space is given to investments in medical and pharmaceutical research. With mission 5, dedicated to social equity, gender and territorial, fundamental will be to invest in effective social and health integration, to offer the right attention and adequate support to fragile and disadvantaged people, such as the lonely elderly, the poor, the homeless, children and the disabled. Another important issue to highlight will be the measures to support the birth rate and the appropriate structural measures for economic and fiscal policy in favour of the family. For all these reasons, the road is set out: strengthening and reorganization of public health, the prevention network, local and local assistance, hospital facilities and their specialties, with an adequate extension of the staff and above all the indispensable connection and interconnection of hospital-territory, an increasingly felt need, which reduces, on the one hand, the access and hospitalizations in hospital and, on the other hand, allows the permanence and follow-up of patients, especially chronic patients, in their family environment and among their affections. We all know how important this is for patients and their therapeutic response, as well as for the family that is experiencing this experience, which often upsets them. The hope and will of all must be that, even from a dramatic and tragic situation such as the one we are experiencing, with all the suffering and thousands of innocent deaths that it has brought, a new way of thinking, organizing and structuring Italian health can be born and started, capable of placing the citizen and his health at the centre and before anything else. We need a change of course and a radical turning point for our country and for us citizens. The problems are many and complex: it is necessary to have the courage, responsibility and firmness to deal with them with the awareness of the choices made. We look with confidence to the future, time will be gallant man and we will be right. Finally, I would like to make a small note in reply to those in these classrooms and squares of our country who have said that very often the measures adopted so far, including social distancing and individual protective devices in the first place, are medieval and anachronistic. In fact, they represent the only internationally recognised scientific measures taken in all countries. They are the only alternatives capable of reducing and preventing the spread of the virus, obviously pending the availability of an effective and safe vaccine. The same must be shared and implemented by all of us independently and without the need for controllers who must constantly monitor their implementation; the latter measures - as we know - completely unenforceable. The sense of responsibility and belonging of all of us is the common good to be shared; defining the guidelines and rules to facilitate the correct application of such measures is the task of those who administer the public thing. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u58	Senator Presutto is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u59	Mr President, distinguished colleagues, representatives of the Government, today we are called to evaluate and vote on the proposal for guidelines for the definition of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan. As we know, the European Council, in order to respond to the crisis generated by the pandemic by Covid-19, has decided to allocate resources of EUR 750 billion, which fall under the Next Generation EU programme, in addition to those provided for in the multiannual financial framework of the European Union for the years 2021-2027, amounting to EUR 1.074.3 billion. The supplementary funds are collected on the markets and are intended to pay particular attention to the future of the new generations, to programmes aimed at promoting the ecological transition and the economic and social recovery of countries affected by the contagion. Of these 750 billion euros, in the last update contained in the Update Note to the Document of Economy and Finance (Nadef) presented in these days, the Government estimates that Italy will go about 205 billion, of which 127 in the form of loan and little less than 80 in the form of a grant lost. Italy, like the other Member States, will have to prepare a National Recovery and Resilience Plan following the guidelines set out by the European Commission, criteria aimed at reducing the gap between countries and taking action to promote their renewal, modernisation and development through an ecological and digital transition, so as to ensure that each individual country, together with its own production system, acquires the necessary capacities to resist new pandemic, social or other economic or natural disasters. The European Commission has established that each country must face, in parallel, both reforms and investments and Italy has proposed in its Recovery and Resilience Plan four important strategic challenges: improving resilience and resilience; reducing the social and economic impact of the pandemic crisis; supporting the green and digital transition; raising the growth potential of the economy and creating jobs. To this end, our country's plan will be articulated in six missions: digitisation, innovation, competitiveness of the productive system; green revolution and ecological transition; mobility infrastructure; education, training, research and culture; social equity, gender and territorial and, not least, health. The six missions, in turn, are divided into clusters, together with homogeneous projects. In line with the European guidelines for a comprehensive investment-related reform package, intervention clusters will be linked to structural reforms of public administration, tax, justice and labour. Ladies and gentlemen, today we are called on to discuss the guidelines for the definition of the Recovery and Resilience Plan, which will lead the Government to draw up the scheme for a rational and organic forecast of investment and reform projects, which will be used to engage in an informal dialogue with the European Commission from 15 October. We have a great opportunity to cope with the current economic crisis and to manage optimally the extraordinary amount of resources available to the European Union. It will be important to ensure the certainty of the timing of the planning and implementation of the projects related to the strategic objectives set out in the guidelines: we owe it to ourselves and to the next generations. The country will have to progress in terms of competitiveness and productivity, with strategic and material infrastructure, such as high-speed and capacity rail connections, and intangible infrastructure, such as the single broadband network to improve the quality of ultra-fast connections proper to a modern and efficient country and, at the same time, respectful of the environment. The European Commission reminds us, however, that the assessment of the Recovery and Resilience Plans presented by individual countries will mainly take into account the specification for each draft quantitative targets relating to agreed indicators covering the resources used and expected results, as well as the intermediate stages of implementation and completion of projects. The provision of European funding for each individual project will be closely linked to the regular achievement of intermediate objectives and milestones. Ladies and gentlemen, at this point, however, it is necessary to bring to your attention some critical issues, which we must urgently address and resolve, in order to make sure that we respect the commitments we will make with the European Union and with our citizens. A few days ago, in this House, we examined the draft laws bearing the General Report of the State Administration for the financial year 2019 and the provisions for the adoption of the State budget for the financial year 2020 and I myself highlighted how some economic and capital parameters of the accounts testify to the historical difficulty of the State in interacting with citizens, both in the collection of revenue and in the implementation of expenditure. These problems are mainly due to public administration based on outdated, ineffective and inefficient management models. However, it will be the latter which will play a central role in the execution of the recovery plan projects. Action will therefore have to be taken to achieve the mother of all reforms, that of the public administration, which will have to adopt a modern organisational and management model, based on processes oriented to the achievement of the objectives, evaluated in the intermediate stages by key performance indicators, in compliance with the criteria of efficiency and the valorization of the concept of functional expenditure and in the perspective of continuous improvement. The modernisation of public administration will necessarily have to go towards the adoption of digital systems, which favour internal interaction between institutions, the interoperability of public databases and interaction with citizens and suppliers. The success of this reform requires trained employees to the new model of work and the new concept of public administration and the selection of suitable professional figures to support and accelerate the cultural change in progress. Only in this way will Italy be able to measure the achievement of the objectives defined in the national recovery and resilience plan and to do so in a preventive manner, bringing any corrective action and even before the European Commission's intervention. Ladies and gentlemen, another aspect that we will have to address is the involvement of local authorities in the implementation of the recovery plan. The Regions have asked to have a say in the matter, but this can only happen in a functional way through the correct application of Title V of the constitutional dictation, which sees the State over-ordered and with a coordinating role with respect to the functions assigned to the Regions. This means that the State will have to ensure the implementation of autonomy and federalism, but respecting the principles of cohesion and solidarity, in order to allow the exercise of civil rights to citizens, without geographical and territorial distinction and can do so through the definition of the essential levels of performance (LEP), both for symmetrical and asymmetrical subjects, which are transferred to the regions that request it. Only in this way and through a careful determination of the equalization fund and the infrastructural equalization, can the economic and social differences between North and South, which are no longer sustainable, as indicated in the recovery plan, be finally filled. At the end of my speech, I would like to put to your attention another vital aspect for the economic recovery of the country, that is the role of the State that, through Cassa depositi e loani, is intervening on strategic assets fundamental for the future of Italy, such as highways, single network, broadband and Italian Stock Exchange. Without going into detail, these specific public interventions, together with others already planned, often in the form of incentives, coordinated by the Government, will serve to create a fertile substrate, on which the private entrepreneurial initiative will be able to plant the seeds for the revival of the Italian economy, which will be oriented to the respect of the environment and the health of the citizens. The combination between the public and the private, with a view to strategic coordination, will facilitate the development of a resilient and competitive economic system, able to face any future adverse events or negative global economic cycles. The National Recovery and Resilience Plan, in its complexity and importance, can be applied to enjoy the expected results, in compliance with the principles and criteria set out by the European Commission, only if the State is able to identify and resolve the critical issues that compromise the functionality of the PA and the interaction with the citizens, to resolve in a clear and precise way the problems of coordination and functional control with the local authorities, to manage the national economic system, balancing the role of the State with the free initiative of private entrepreneurship. One thing is certain: it is only thanks to the Government and the constant support provided by the Movimento 5 Stars, that today we are able to discuss and outline a great project for the country. The amount of resources coming must make us feel the full weight of this responsibility, but it must also give us the enthusiasm and awareness of having achieved, thanks to the recovery fund, a decisive result, which until a few months ago, in accounting and arithmetic Europe, would have simply been impossible to imagine. Today it is reality: we must look forward, in the conviction that Italy will soon be able to return to release all its immense potentialities. [[Applause]]

In conclusion, buffer interventions are not enough; we need an overall project of structural change that addresses the criticalities exploded with the Covid, but that we already knew to be well present before, and that have been blamefully ignored. We need a model that holds together protection and emancipation, that returns to give confidence, certainty and protagonism: it is the sense of this plan. ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u66	She's enlisted to speak Senator Garnet. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u67	Mr President, this is a unique and unrepeatable opportunity, which Europe offers us today, to benefit from almost EUR 209 billion of loans, of which EUR 80 billion is lost. Italy is the country that has benefited from the largest share. The European Commission, with the Next Generation EU, has allocated a total of €750 billion to overcome the economic crisis resulting from the pandemic, through a National Recovery and Resilience Plan. In the allocation of EUR 750 billion between Member States, it has defined three criteria: population, per capita income and average unemployment over the last five years. According to these criteria, Italy has been allocated 209 billion euros, the most important slice of the total amount. If the criterion had been only that of the population, Italy would have received only €97,5 billion, or less than half. The remaining share - EUR 111.5 billion - was allocated to Italy because the Mezzogiorno has an average per capita income of 17,000 euros compared to 33,000 in the Centre-North and an unemployment rate of 17% compared to 7.6 percent in the Centre-North. I would like to say this in order to alert you to the real risk that once again, despite the recommendations of the European Union and the reassuring declarations of intent, the South, because of the delays in planning, remains behind. We know, in fact, that most of the infrastructure essential to the South to bridge the gap and relaunch the development of the territories is in a state of extremely backward design, so it is essential that effective measures be identified as quickly as possible to speed up the planning and authorisation procedures, indispensable to make available immediately those resources also for the Mezzogiorno. It is essential that the identification of the destination of the resources of the recovery fund follows the criterion for equalization and, therefore, action is taken for the South with particular regard to those sectors which suffer a condition of net disadvantage. I am referring specifically to mobility infrastructure, public health, school building, university facilities. In the context of mission 4 (Education, Training, Research and Culture), given that Council Recommendation 2 on the National Reform Programme of Italy 2019 called on our Government to take measures to improve school outcomes, including through appropriate targeted investment, and to promote skills improvement, public expenditure on education, research and development must be increased in order to bridge the expenditure gap in relation to GDP vis-à-vis the average of the EU countries, so as to place Italy above that level in the time frame of the Next Generation EU programme. The proposals identified provide for the strengthening of policies for school inclusion and the fight against abandonment and dispersion, through tools to support disability and fragility. School staff are the most paid and encouraged staff of the public administration, the oldest and most precarious; therefore, it is necessary to invest in training in the service of teaching and administrative staff that is paid and in strengthening, increasing and developing the laboratory activities of technical and professional institutions. In addition, a strong investment is needed to improve the teaching of STEM disciplines (science, technology, engineering and mathematics), in which Italy has the highest training gap compared to other European countries. Specific economic incentives must be planned for the profession of teachers, on which the quality levels of the service provided depend. Further action is also needed for inclusion and against school dispersal and educational poverty. I refer, in particular, to the increase in the legal staff on support, which is no longer a matter of referral; to the reduction in the number of pupils per class; to the strengthening of public provision in the zero-six-year segment, through the adoption of a school building plan for the establishment of state centres for children; to the integration of the training offer in primary school, through the inclusion of musical and choreutic paths in full time, and to the introduction of the specialized teacher of motor sciences. Moreover, investments in school and university infrastructure in terms of energy efficiency, anti-seismic and technological modernisation are of strategic importance. Investment in research and development, as well as in measures for the right to study, must help to increase the number of graduates, who are currently well below the European average. These will have to ensure that young researchers are included in appropriate career growth paths, including innovative ones, in public or private research centres. To this end, it is also appropriate to invest in higher technical education by strengthening the network of technical training in all regions and territories of the country and supporting the technical-scientific pathways also for the post-graduate specialization for professionalism of close connection with the world of work and university. In addition, actions are essential to encourage foreign researchers to carry out research activities in Italy, also with the investment on the best ideas in all disciplines for research projects in the artistic and musical fields. Information and communication technology needs to focus on data infrastructures, artificial intelligence, computer security, blockchain applications, quantum mechanics. Particular attention should be paid to the development and dissemination of environmental research and development, accelerating the acquisition of skills and the dissemination of technologies to support the green transition. These are central challenges for the economic and social development of the country, which cannot miss this historic opportunity to lead the process of scientific and technological innovation on which all the most advanced countries are investing. In the medical field it is necessary to introduce measures to overcome the training funnel between the degree course in medicine and the schools of specialization; to recalibrate the closed number in the faculties with programmed access, especially scientific and, in the light of the experiences linked to the pandemic, to relaunch national schools of specialization of general medicine and emergencies of the territory. In terms of culture, we must relaunch the concept of widespread culture as a development key for our country, strengthening the link between the protection and enhancement of cultural heritage and environmental protection in the context of a new sustainable development in which the potential of technologies applied to the field of cultural goods and activities are developed. In particular, appropriate resources will have to be allocated for the recovery and enhancement of the extraordinary treasures of the small villages, promoting the structure of tourism more widespread and diluted throughout the national territory and not concentrated only on the cities of art. Indispensable is also the planning of interventions to prevent the damage caused by climate change on the historical, artistic, monumental and archaeological heritage. On the cultural front, the workers of the entertainment world were among the most penalized by the restrictions imposed and by the anti-Covid prevention measures. Therefore, it is now improcrastinable that a statute of the work of the arts is put into place in line with the provisions of the European Parliament resolution of 7 June 2007 on the social statute of artists in order to ensure adequate social protection and shock absorbers for all workers in the world of culture, entertainment and performing art, in line with what has been defined by the emerging measures adopted so far. Finally, in the field of sport, also with a view to overall urban regeneration and the regeneration of cities, resources must be invested for the redevelopment and construction of sports facilities at the service of schools, but open to the territorial community, especially in the urban suburbs, in order to promote social inclusion, recovery of degraded socio-economic contexts and dissemination of sports and motor practice to all, with particular regard to disability. Realizing this ambitious program of economic, educational and social reforms through the extraordinary resources that we will have available will mean starting our country towards a path of growth and recovery that, due to restrictions, austerity policies and fiscal compact, up to now seemed to be definitively foreclosed. A critical issue, therefore, has turned into a great opportunity. [[Applause.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u69	Mr President, GDP will fall by 10% in 2020.
