These are percentage numbers, cold figures that can already be impressed on paper but that are dramatic in real life: they mean 410,000 people who have lost or will lose their jobs by the end of this year; a dizzying public debt that risks being irrecoverable if there is no economic breakthrough, a debt that will weigh like a boulder on the future of the nation and on the new generations; an average growth rate of 0.8% of the Italian economy in the last decade compared to a European average of 1.6%; a research and development expenditure of 1.3% against a European average of 2.1; an employment rate of 10 percentage points below the European average: 73.2% versus 63 percent of Italy; public investment below 3% of GDP. This, ladies and gentlemen, is the economic framework in which we are moving, not at all reassuring, which puts us on a dangerous ridge if we cannot make the right choices in this which may be the last chance not for the government or the majority holding but for the nation. We are talking about over EUR 200 billion, a loan part and a grant part. 70% must be committed in the years 2021 and 2022 and the remaining 30 by 2023: an important figure to be committed in a relatively short time. From here comes the first concern: in the face of a historical criticality in spending European resources totally, the doubt arises that we will have probable difficulties in presenting projects and committing resources. But even more we are concerned about the little capacity of this Government to realize in serious projects the guidelines of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan: 100 billion extraordinary resources, resources equal to some budget laws, many of which have been squandered in inadequate actions, in the decrees care Italy, relaunching, liquidity and finally the August decree; non-structural actions but based on a general welfare aim in itself, an endless series of bonuses and waste of resources that have hardly served. With this measure we speak, yes, of the future but the present is the real drama: workers who still have not received the Cassa Integrazione, professionals and artisans who have not received any contribution and the famous bazooka of the liquidity decree has proved to be a real failure: entire production sectors zeroed and I am thinking in particular of tourism. The school system is in disarray with a minister unable to handle the situation, without talking about justice and the prison system. The infrastructure system is now in collapse. But despite all this, despite your citizenship incomes, your bankruptcy sanatories, we can do it if - and I repeat if - you abandon your palace positions and return to living in the real country. The guidelines of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan are a list of good intentions, but they do not outline a precise structural recovery plan, and I speak of Nation, unlike many other colleagues who talk about the country. I would like to focus on something that is particularly dear to me, given my role in the Commission, namely agriculture. Everyone has noted that the agricultural system, during the closure of the activities due to Covid, has supported even if with enormous efforts, but the sector suffers from lack of attention, suffers from the lack of a vision plan for the future, suffers from lack of resources. I would remind you that of the EUR 100 billion put in place by the government decrees, just under two and a half billion have been made available directly to the agricultural sector. Even today, we cannot see, through the general lines of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan (NPNR), a direct action for Italian agriculture, so much so that in the opinion expressed by the rapporteur in the Committee on Agriculture we read: "it is worthwhile the Commission's merit to include, in the draft report, a chapter specifically on the strategy for relaunching the agricultural sector, forestry and fisheries" and then to include a few dozen comments. In view of the final document - and I say this to my colleagues in the Commission - you have disappointed yourself. Although the document recognises the centrality of the primary sector of agriculture, there is no specific chapter, but interventions are distributed between different competences and this could certainly frustrate the impact on the agricultural sector. We therefore need a single planning for agriculture. I am thinking of the digital development of agriculture, expanding broadband also in those areas considered marginal. Of course, it will then have to be accompanied by appropriate training, starting also from schools as well as from specialized subjects. This could also promote the generational revival that is becoming an emergency in agriculture. An agriculture that can rely on technology also means better exploitation of soil, water resources, less consumption of chemicals and therefore greater optimization of production and better quality. How can we not then think of infrastructure investments in order to upgrade and optimize the logistics for the transport of the goods? It is necessary to promote the internal areas with a strong agricultural vocation, especially the mountain and hilly ones. The abandonment of those areas has an impact not only on agricultural activities, but also on the entire environmental system, from landscape care to the prevention of hydrogeological failure. We need a sustainability plan against the excessive presence of wildlife. Last but not least, food supply chains must be encouraged as a strategic axis for the growth of the national economy. We need resources and investments in some key areas of agri-food, with clear policies for the enhancement and protection of Italian products, greater protection of our brands of excellence from counterfeits that cause a loss to the sector of hundreds of millions of euros. We cannot forget the promotion, which must be encouraged and coordinated in order not to disperse resources and opportunities. I will conclude, Mr President, by saying that we are at a crossroads: we can only take the road to relaunch if we succeed in strengthening the production system, placing companies at the centre of political action and giving confidence to those who do business. We need to relaunch investment. We must make courageous and structural reforms of the country system and not demagogic reforms just to plant a political flag. We have to think about the future of the nation and not about the next political elections like this majority does, otherwise we risk dragging the nation into a coffin from which it will not be able to trace back. The Group of Brothers of Italy, as hitherto has always shown, as a patriotic force, will always be present to defend national interests, but our patience, like that of Italians and farmers, is ending. So no to assistantism, no to rain contributions, no to statism. They are all infrastructures, as well as digital ones, without which we cannot talk about development in the South or even a reversal of the trend towards the whole country. You have to spend it now. This means that for real, and not for fiction, it is necessary to cut the Italian bureaucracy with a stroke of darkness. The Italians would have done so very willingly if only this Government had given the Italian people the opportunity to do so! The issue of waste management obviously involves the question of their reduction, as well as the adaptation and coordination of waste management policies on a national basis. Even today in every region, in every province and I would say in every city, according to the manager, we have different ways of managing the separate collection; in one place the tetrapak is thrown into the paper, in another place in the plastic and in another still in the mixture. First of all, we need to rationalise the issue of separate collection, in order to promote the circular economy and to benefit territories and citizens. There is also the theme of the connection between waste management, especially the wet fraction, and the water cycle; from this connection important benefits can be generated in terms of circular economy, through the production of natural gas, which obviously impacts further on the economic system. Mr President, with regard to the issue of energy redevelopment of buildings, I think it will be very important to direct the resources that we will have available to strengthen the ecobonus by 11% and the earthquakebonus mainly towards public housing. In each region there is a huge amount of so-called popular houses - be they regional or municipal - that need almost everywhere very important redevelopment interventions. Let's start with energy redevelopment: we also give mayors and regional presidents the opportunity to use the 110% ecobonus to restore this great heritage of our country. The same applies to schools. I also quickly touch the theme of the reclamations of the disused areas. Many of the urban regeneration interventions that have to do with abandoned areas cannot continue, they cannot be carried out because they lack adequate resources to do the reclamation. In this case, too, we use the resources that will be made available to us in Europe to give a boost to the recovery of these areas, of these areas and thus to promote the economic recovery of many territories. However, it was not necessary to fall into the conceptual deception of digital competence. Knowing how to use technology does not mean knowing how to use it well, without harming oneself and others, because the difference between digital competence and digital education is not so trivial, and the consequences of diseducation, or even worse than a digital maleducation, can be very dramatic. We go from technological dependencies of various kinds to so-called digital dementia, to cyberbullying, which generates victims every day among our young people, to different forms of cybercrime. In order for digitalization to truly represent a resource for our country and to be an instrument at our service and never - and I say never - the opposite, i.e. an instrument of slavery and dehumanization, we will need to prepare society, building a training designed precisely to create that education to a correct use of technology and the Network in general. Digital education will have to reach our schools, but also in the world of work, among professionals of all generations, in order to involve not only professionals, but even those who, among them, are parents, because a correct parenting example can help the whole of society by transmitting the culture of self-respect, of the other and of the world around us, to give all the parents of Italy the courage to educate: only in this way can we make this world a better place for our next generation. Last but not least, I would like to recall the acceptance of the proposal on resilience training, which obviously goes for education to respect, for emotional literacy and training to empathy, with the aim of curbing, containing, but above all preventing the dramatic phenomena of bullying, cyberbullying and gender violence, the discrimination that has made our youngest social fabric bleed for too long now. There is a time to sow, paying attention to that every sod of land available gives the maximum of the fruits that it is able to produce. This is our time to sow, and this is what we have done, working together today, preparing all the appropriate tools to promote and increase the chances of recovery of our country to the advantage of Italians. This is the main task of those who want to make politics: to educate the community in the values of altruism and solidarity. It is the only way to achieve a peaceful cultural revolution. The political offer cannot be limited to denouncing the disaster that the spread of the pandemic has created in society, but it must offer a concrete vision of change that looks at man in his natural social dimension and that seeks to break down the barriers of disintegration and individualism. The parent is respected and loved by his children when he incarnated an altruistic cultural model, taking care of all their material and existential needs, never appearing as a despot, but only and always as a certainty on which to rely. The same goes for politics, which today has taken care of its citizens, accompanying them towards the post-Covid era, with self-denial, far-sightedness and responsibility. For these reasons, I am voting in favour of this document. [[Applause.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u82	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Minister, one of the strategic challenges facing the National Recovery and Resilience Plan is the digital transition of our country. This objective, in order to be centered, needs to be declined in the way and in what to do. We know that digitisation will bring positive reflections of undoubted value: investment in digital can increase the rate of growth and even double it; can provide new jobs, going to increase the employment rate for high-skilled professionals and more. Digital development also improves the BES indicators and reduces territorial gaps. We already have many examples of workers who have moved back to our southern areas, while maintaining their employment in London or Amsterdam, because they are high-paid jobs that bring wealth to our South Italy. These work are environmentally sustainable, because they reduce the need for travel, commuting and consequently traffic and pollution. These works, requiring better school preparation, especially in the degrees in science, technology, engineering and mathematics (STEM), contribute to raising the country's cultural level. Given this, when it is said to focus investment on the digital transition, how can we achieve the objective? Firstly, action is needed on public administration, which in an innovative state can act as a driver for digitisation. I would like to thank Commissions 5a and 14a for accepting in the final document the opinion drawn up by the 1st Commission, which highlights the important actions for public administration. The powers of the public administration are fundamental. It will have to recruit a new qualified management to design procedures natively computer science; moreover it is necessary to reverse the outsourcing policy of the public administration, that has produced emptying of computer skills and prevented the generational turnover. A new recruitment policy is needed to bring IT management back into the public administration, consisting of staff with their advanced digital skills and leading industry professionals with proven titles and experience. Software programmers, system planners, data analysts, network technicians, IT security engineers must be recruited with an appropriate remuneration at market level. The aim must be to recruit the best professionalism, linking them to the service and mission proper to the public administration, also in view of the return of the brains and the new recruitment from foreign countries. The question of competences in public administration is fundamental, because digitisation is not done with the magic wand, but with the experience and skills of this kind of professional. Part of the Next generation EU funds must be used for this new workforce. Unfortunately, there is a shortage of these skills throughout Europe. The advanced countries hunt these brains all over the world and also in Italy; so our new graduates STEM (science, technology, engineering and mathematics), who easily find jobs abroad, leave our country empty of these skills. As part of STEM training, we have two problems: a low number of graduates and a recruitment system that does not value them as abroad. We must act in both directions. In the first place, the mathematical, scientific and digital skills of both adults and children need to be increased, without gender distinction. It is also necessary to improve the system of acquisition of skills, starting with the training of teachers. School programs must be changed from elementary to elementary: once in elementary school they were taught to compile a telegram, today they must teach the instruments of individual productivity and computational thought. In addition to those on the human capital front of the public administration, the actions to be carried out in the short term concern digital infrastructures. Next generation EU funds must be invested in the implementation of the national cloud, under a dual guideline: on the one hand, the creation of the national strategic hub, i.e. the private cloud of public administration, as foreseen in the simplification decree, which must constitute the safe of the most valuable data of public administration; on the other hand, the creation of a large public-private partnership for the realization of the Italian public cloud, to which all Italian companies with proven competence in the sector participate, which will become the country's cloud infrastructure, on which all the services and applications created by companies, startups, but also by public administration, school, the world of university and research will be based. The very realization of the artificial intelligence strategy can only be based on the existence of a national cloud, so as not to reduce ourselves to mere consumers of artificial intelligence, but to aim for an active role in Europe in this area. Finally, there is a need to speed up the completion of ultra-wideband infrastructure. Therefore, ultra-wide band, national cloud, advanced digital skills represent the essential infrastructure for our country, on which is based a serious policy for the digital and technological sovereignty that Italy must implement, integrating it wisely within the framework of the European dimension. From these bases you can begin to build, for all state, regional and local administrations, interoperable services and platforms, remotely accessible and suitable for smart working. It is therefore necessary to recognise the State's competence to coordinate public administrations' infrastructure and IT platforms in order to coordinate and ensure the interoperability of systems and data. Services must be natively digital and allow a unified communication channel between citizens and public administrations, giving full implementation to the principle called once-only. The path takes place in three steps: firstly, existing databases are coordinated at national level and data are made available through the national data platform. Secondly, using this data, services are provided through the production of innovative software and, finally, the citizen is given access to services through connectivity and digital identity. Thanks to these enabling infrastructures, professionals will develop the innumerable digital services we need, for example, in the health system, in the judicial, in the school system, in the social security, in the tax, tax and financial fields, in agriculture, in transport, in tourism and more widely in the cultural sector for monitoring the ex post execution of projects and works and in every area of human action. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, at this difficult time for the world and for Italy we have an opportunity to exploit which could enable us to eliminate many of the delays that affect our economic and social system. We must be willing to make investments and, in particular, on human capital, which are necessary to make Italy competitive with our European and international partners. I am convinced that we will not waste these opportunities and we will overcome this ambitious, challenging and fundamental challenge for our future and that of our children. [[Applause.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u83	Senator Nencini is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u84	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Mr Amendola, I will not go into the substance of the proposals, except for the broad lines that the Education Commission has drawn up, distinguishing them between recommendations and observations, precisely to give the Government a road that can be carried out more carefully. I shall try, on the other hand, in the minutes that the Presidency gives me, to define, from my point of view, a framework in which Italy - I appreciated the reports this morning - should be placed. I am pleased to recall that the policies to which the Italian Government is working must be understood as a piece of a mosaic that concerns the other countries of the European Union and not as a single, individual and customizable fact. The reason lies in the fact that, beyond the many declarations of intent made during the lockdown that each of us would have come out of quarantine better, in the sense of better, and globalization would have stopped of enchantment, as if a historical process started more or less five hundred years ago can be blocked by fifty, sixty days of quarantine, that is the goal in which to place these policies, if we do not want the prophecy of several years ago that had to make Spengler on the sunset of the West not to come about. I would like to think that the policies of Italy in the heart of the European Union, together with the other countries of the European Union, in managing such massive investments are intended, on the one hand, to correct globalisation as we have known it, in fact driven by high finance and multinational companies, and, on the other hand, to try to build a sort of humanised state and, therefore, of the more humanised European Union able to make welfare a new social state at the heart of the new attentions of the Union in order to participate in the competitive challenge already opened by fifteen years between China and the United States of America. I remember two factors that are constant in history: first in the moments of change, although children of the emergency (the emergency may be, for example, a pandemic or a war) prepares a further change. The second constant is that change prepares for transition, so individual or state leadership prepares for such a phase. It is the reason why in the guidelines that are delivered to us, on which the Commissions have worked, there is no doubt that the four fields that bind with a single thread and which go under the name of education, culture, knowledge and innovation, must be at the center of attention together with the policies of the new green. Being at the center of attention means, as an old peasant motto used to say, that those who know more words always win. Here, also in the telematic society, in the society overpoweringly computerized and linked to a constant technological revolution, there is no doubt that those who know more words always win. I would like to add two points, Mr President, and finish. In the opinion that the Commission that I chair has issued, presenting it to the Government, there are three points around which the proposals put forward revolve. The first point concerns civic and social reorganization, the second concerns human relations and the third concerns the abandonment of bonuses to work on the issue of opportunities and therefore on investments related to opportunities to escape from the previous logic that I understand was indispensable at least in the months of spring. The five points that have been declined here I remember only for chapter: urban patchwork, enhancement of landscape and historic villages, knowledge, innovation and protection (because there is still a world completely devoid of protection and I think of the world of entertainment, culture and sport). Around these five points we have built working hypotheses. To deliver them, however, it is necessary to tie them under two conditions. As is the case at times of emergency, or as should be the case, oppositions should be involved and institutionally involved. We faced the bends of history in Italy in two different ways: at the end of World War I in one way and at the end of World War II in the opposite way. I keep thinking the opposite way is the right way. My second and final consideration concerns the involvement of Parliament, Minister Amendola, in all that will be the monitoring of the projects that the Government will present, for many reasons but above all because, Minister, from the conditions of emergency - pandemic, war or anything else - comes out above all by recreating a kind of civic spirit that involves even before the institutions the Italians, if you can all stay "at the stand" and create a condition of solidarity and above all hope. To do so, you have to be overwhelmed and pioneers: weird to keep an eye today and an eye on tomorrow, pioneers to put yourself at risk. These are the two essential conditions to emerge from the crisis. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u87	Senator De Bonis is enlisted to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u88	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the opportunity given to us by the Next Generation EU and the National Recovery and Resilience Plan certainly does not happen every day. The approximately 200 billion euros that would arrive to Italy, between loans and grants lost, could be the opportunity, not only to recover from the crisis generated by the emergency health, but also to put our socio-economic system back in place and then put it back on track. To do this, however, we need a strong political determination, acting within the framework of a strategic vision that is not limited to individual interventions but establishes, once and for all, what kind of country we want to become. I am convinced, and all my institutional acts are there to testify, that the direction to be taken is that of a sustainable, green, biological Italy and finally free from a territorial gap that has lasted too long and is based on a major historical misunderstanding. As far as the agricultural sector is concerned, it is essential to focus attention on large-scale projects and strategic vision, rather than on micro-sectoral interventions. Just as it is essential for Italy to present detailed draft guidelines as soon as possible, no later than next January. On the other hand, eco-sustainable projects which have little or nothing to do with the green revolution and which are seriously at risk of being rejected would waste valuable time, even in Europe. That is why I want to point out a number of objectives on which interventions should be concentrated in order to ensure that agriculture really takes up its historical role, as an economic driving force and as an environmental, landscape and cultural garrison. Firstly, the promotion of the agri-food, sustainable and organic sectors must be seriously encouraged, while at the same time discouraging supply chains that rely on chemicals and pollutants. In this context, by promoting quality food, it would also contribute to the fight against food waste and to the reduction of the health budget. In addition, action must be taken to make water resources truly efficient and to improve the capacity of the production and agricultural system to adapt to climate change. Further, action is needed to retrain the territory in the context of the containment of soil consumption and the mitigation of hydrogeological and seismic risks. I would also like to stress the fact that ecological conversion, on which the Government itself agrees, can only take place through the strong impetus for organic and sustainable agriculture, particularly in the Mezzogiorno. This can also be achieved by stimulating higher education in this field, for example by providing universities specialising in organic farming, which are currently lacking. Technological innovation projects in the agri-food and fisheries sector must also be financed, provided that this does not mean intervention aimed at "Frankenstein" agriculture, based on a view of nature as if it were a cow to milk. Furthermore, measures must certainly be put in place to safeguard agricultural production potential, including by avoiding further consumption of agricultural land, and a national plan for the sustainable transition process involving structural measures at enterprise and value chain level, complemented by national plans for bioeconomy, organic farming and the circular economy (we have also discussed this in the Commission). It is also useful to set up a centralized programme for the complete traceability of supply chains, but relying on institutions and universities that already do research in this area, without uncomfortableing Sogin, for example. It is important to encourage the adoption of zonal plans to develop water management and land recovery measures in order to increase the production capacity of the agricultural system and to reduce our dependence on imports (e.g. cereals). Sustainable and productive forest management (I am thinking, for example, of the development of truffles) must also be implemented through investments aimed at the maintenance and redevelopment of the forest and mountain areas, as well as introducing forms of economic support for enterprises in the agricultural and fisheries sectors that invest in sustainable production systems and eliminating incentives for practices such as sowing on soil, which make use of haddock and which are encouraged in many regions. Rural roads must be improved, with particular reference to mountain and inland areas, to promote access for young farmers to the land, to promote food and environmental education campaigns for proper nutrition and to counter food waste, to ensure decent and quality food, produced with mainly Italian raw materials and free of contaminants. Now I would like to highlight another game on which we are playing the future of our country and on which we cannot throw this historic opportunity into the wind: I am referring to the heavy gap between the North and the South of our country. The criteria for a fair distribution of the recovery fund, according to the parameters indicated by the European Union, are designed to reduce disparities between the different national territories, relying on economic interdependence. All this is fully consistent with what should be the priorities in Italy. According to the indications of the European Union, it is clear that the South is responsible for 70% of the resources provided for in the recovery fund. The EUR 208 billion that will be made available to Italy has been calculated on the basis of three factors: resident population, per capita income and unemployment rate. Following these criteria and accepting the recommendation to seek greater integration between North and South, the largest slice should go to the South, in order to bridge this gap. However, this unique opportunity must be managed at its best, with an overall and organic view of the type of development expected. Agriculture and sustainable tourism, transport to connect the isolated areas of the hinterland, support for small and medium-sized enterprises and artisans, public health, school, environment: these are the games in which you will not have to go back a step to restore dignity to the Mezzogiorno and make it the epicentre of a new idea of Italian and European progress. With the recovery fund there is finally an opportunity to remedy an injustice that has lasted for a century and a half: a country split in half, in which the Mezzogiorno has been systematically taken away resources and, with them, the very possibility of the future, as the territorial public accounts testify widely. Unfortunately the dominant propaganda would like to convince that there is a wasteful and washable South, but the truth lies in an inequality that has lasted for decades. Therefore, European funds - and I am going to conclude - will have to be wisely distributed; in order to really balance the existing gap the rule of 34% is no longer sufficient. The parameters indicated by Europe say that, if out of 100 euros invested in the Mezzogiorno 41 euros go to the North, out of 100 euros invested in the North 5 euros go to the South. So the goal is to rebalance and what is needed is to focus funding on the weakest part of the country. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u89	Senator Nugnes is registered to speak. You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u90	Mr President, the opinion of the 13th Commission on the guidelines of the government is full-bodied and could not be otherwise, since 37% of the large investments for each country will have to concern the ecological transition. There are many issues dealt with in this opinion, such as stop to the consumption of soil, hydrogeological safety, waste, circular economy, the transition from environmentally harmful subsidies (SAD) to environmentally favourable subsidies (SAF), transport, energy. It is precisely on the energy that I want to dwell on, because on this we play a great game. We are starting from the old Integrated National Energy and Climate Plan (Pniac): let me say this because it is a document that actually looks extremely old today and - I regret to say - it already appeared so when it was drawn up, with the forecast of a 30% reduction in emissions and a gas-based energy transition, which would have brought us to 2030 again with 65% of hydrocarbons out of total energy consumption. Gas, we know, is a highly climatic element, which contributes to the ozone hole. Already with the green new deal, which has given itself more ambitious targets, with emission reductions around 55%, we had to some extent imposed a revision of the document, which today finds us even more bound, because it must be consistent with the objectives of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan (PNRR) post-pandemic. It is therefore necessary to stress and reiterate, as Europe has already said, as the 13th European Council has done. The Commission and - I am pleased to see - as was also the view of the Commissions 5a and 14a, which must be directed, as well as the electricity strictly from clean and renewable energy sources, at hydrogen green, also in order to align Italy with the projects already in place in the rest of Europe - as in France and Germany, which have invested large sums on this sector, reminding us that we also have an advantage, that we can produce hydrogen from the sun and not necessarily only from the wind - but, above all, to free ourselves from this gas transition. It is therefore time to tell us that hydrogen from renewable sources is a reality to which we can entrust ourselves. There is, however, a "ma": you have to be careful about fossil dinosaurs, as defined in an e-book of October's "Manifesto." The fossil dinosaurs, in fact, return to the attack for the defense of their own interests, proposing hydrogen as a gas vector at 5 or 10 percent (just see the ENI and SNAM projects) and this in order not to give up the gas fields that are in the Mediterranean and on which they are doing great battle to contend for the management. Why are they talking about hydrogen, too? Because hydrogen is on the ridge of the wave, ready to take off the true energy transition. So we have to somehow tame it, bend it to interests. However, hydrogen does not need to be a gas carrier, and that is very clear, partly because the European Commission finally published the expected hydrogen strategy on 8 July, which complements the new industrial strategy of March (it is part of the green new deal package), which has the zero emission target at 2050. It states unequivocally that the priority must be given to green hydrogen, that produced from renewable sources, while discarding hydrogen from fossil sources, giving just an opening to the so-called blue hydrogen, the gas that should capture CO2 and seize it in a process called CCS. This escamotage does not convince, first because it is not proven the economic convenience with respect to hydrogen green and, if it is not tried today, it will certainly be proven exactly the opposite in four or five years, when it has been predicted that the hydrogen will go from 4 or 5 dollars per kilogram, to 2 dollars per kilogram, thus becoming competitive also with diesel. Compared to the type of hydrogen we have to think about, because I often hear about neutral technology, but technology is not, because it always has consequences. In fact, it happened that on 24 June, GasNaturally, a coalition of fossil companies, including ENI, pushed for the CCS (carbon capture and storage) technique to be considered a clean hydrogen. Thus the official document of 8 July was slightly modified, with a cautious opening also to blue hydrogen. However, we must not forget that already in 2007 the European Union, in the climate-energy package 20-20-20, invested more than EUR 1 billion in the creation of twelve demonstration plants, in the commercial production of electricity, with capture and storage of CO2, of which no news is yet available. All of this is certified by the report of a special Commission of the European Parliament. Therefore, I believe that experiments and investments in this area have already been made, that the time is ripe for hydrogen green and that there is no longer any discussion: we must proceed in this direction. [[Applause]]

A series of reforms are indicated: there is talk of reform of justice and health; I have heard that there is talk of an ecological transition, with a whole revolutionary and innovative movement prodromic to an excellent life of excellence, and that it truly favours a new way of living Italy and to be modern and projected towards a system that finally allows us to be like other European states. Too bad we lack two fundamental reforms. The first is that of the public administration. In fact, it is not by inserting new professional figures in the bodies responsible for carrying out a design that resolves the issues. It takes a reform of the public administration and one of the contract code, which is fundamental; I understand that I am repetitive, but everything passes from there, otherwise it will be a funnel. The undersigned has submitted a bill, the Senate Act n. 1805, titled "Moratory in the matter of the code of public contracts. Direct application of the provisions of Directive 2014/24/EU and Communication 2020/C 108 I/01 of the European Commission for Public Procurement of Services and Supplies, which was regularly assigned to the 8th Senate Commission on 26 May 2020, but has never been incardinated and has passed almost six months. If we had approved it, we would have had a reform, because the text was to be filled with content, it was not the Beech Law. But kindly yesterday evening, following our very brief hearing (three were held and yesterday's hearing was important), the following words were given on page 24 of the report: in particular, the regulatory framework of the Procurement Code will have to be revised. In conclusion, having said this here and also to you, Mr President, in your role, and hoping that the President of the 8th Commission will also be present, I hope that the aforementioned draft law will now be incardinated, which will give the possibility to implement a simplification in order to carry out those 567 projects, because everything goes from there. I'm waiting confidently. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u98	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, representatives of the Government, I begin my speech with a quote: the European recovery fund must be used for a recovery that responds to the risks of climate change with renewable energy and other technologies, thus opening the opportunity for the creation of a huge number of jobs. Robots and artificial intelligence cannot modernise buildings and all other infrastructures; they need to be human. For Italy it may be the beginning of a new Renaissance. These are the words of Jeremy Rifkin, taken from an interview published yesterday in a well-known national newspaper. Rifkin, as we all know, is an economist, environmentalist and also a very active writer on issues relating to climate change, technologies and global change and has been a consultant for Europe since the early 2000s. His interview is very interesting not only for the topics addressed, such as the green new deal, but also because you are told about the enormous potential that our country has. Rifkin mentions the great talents that our country has in the field of communications, energy, logistics and transport and also our natural resources: you are Saudi Arabia of the sun and the wind, he says; only the political will is missing: it will come from the young people who understand, he adds. On the point of lack of political will, I believe that we could open up an important debate here, because there are probably some things that are not being taken in the general sense, but that are still taking place. The 110 percent superbonus is a revolution and represents a new way of thinking about work, energy and the environment. It goes exactly in the direction that Rifkin also asks: this is the first time the State has invested in citizens, asking them to use the best technologies to save energy consumption and it does so by rewarding them economically. In this way, we get environmental benefits, retrain our work, bringing it to the highest level, and we also encourage companies to use and create new technologies. Again, I am thinking of the work that was done by the Ministry of the Environment and the Protection of the Territory and the Sea on environmentally harmful subsidies (SAD); we had foreseen this work in the last budget law and now that aid will be converted into environmentally favourable subsidies. I stop here with the examples, but I am convinced that since the Movimento 5 Stars arrived to the Government of Italy many things have changed in a favourable way for the environment and this is before everyone's eyes. [[Applause]] To the young people, who are sending us very important messages, I say that they have certainly not been unheard. Here, in this House, two motions on climate change have been adopted. In these days our Commissions have done a huge job to expand and develop the fundamental points for the National Recovery and Resilience Plan, using the resources available to Italy for a single, great goal: the protection of our territories and the 60 million citizens who live there. As other colleagues have also reminded us and as we are all asked, one of the three pillars of the government's recovery plan is the ecological transition. It will be necessary, therefore, to graft a new paradigm into the economy of this country, that puts the environment at the center of every choice. The images of these days (bridges swept away as if they were paper-mâché, submerged cities, rivers that overflow) are snapshots that we will not easily erase from our memory. The task of politics - our task, ladies and gentlemen - is to work for the public, for the good of every citizen. The Movimento 5 Stars has worked a lot on the guidelines under discussion today, moving forward the idea that nothing was left to chance, putting the environment at the centre. Let me say that, rather than talking about protection, in this case I would talk about respect for the environment. We have included some general demands, which give an orientation on the objectives to be achieved with the National Recovery and Resilience Plan and also some fundamental criteria for us to choose and evaluate projects. Part of the funds will have to be devoted to the prevention of hydrogeological failure and seismic risk; it is also necessary to aim at zeroing soil consumption. Projects for the circular economy will be fundamental, with incentives for companies to invest in the conversion of their activities or in the design of products that can be more easily reused or recycled. It is also necessary to support research and experimentation, aimed at promoting end of waste processes. It will be essential to stabilize the green bonuses, from those dedicated to sustainable mobility up to the 110% superbonus for the energy efficiency of buildings. We have requested that, within the recovery plan, the National Waste Management Plan be fully incorporated in order to achieve the objectives set out in the European directives, keeping the waste hierarchy firmly in place, limiting, until it is eliminated, the disposal of waste in landfills or incinerators. There is a need to tackle with a systemic approach the problem related to water cycle management, which needs an overall and adequate view of investments and interventions, ensuring the implementation of an adequate system of installations and measures for the treatment of waste and water. Such a plan would have a major impact on employment and development, which would result in a better quality of life for all. It is thought that in Italy there are still territories without purification plants. It will also be crucial to join forces to protect the marine environment and the coastal strip, to preserve and defend natural habitats and to protect biodiversity. It will also be important to direct our agriculture towards the model from the producer to the consumer. Agriculture, too, needs to be able to follow circular models based on regeneration and the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions. [[Applause]] It is necessary to encourage actions for the sustainability of food production, for the security of supply, to stimulate virtuous practices in the areas of food processing, wholesale and retail trade, hotel and catering services. Correct food consumption must be promoted and the transition to healthy and sustainable food regimes facilitated, as well as waste reduction. As stated yesterday in Taranto by the Minister for Economic Development Stefano Patuanelli, Italy also needs social regeneration plans. It is therefore necessary to combat social marginality, cultural deprivation, school dispersal and juvenile deviance, through material and intangible actions. These themes, which are of a social nature, are reflected on the environment. In fact, where these social problems remain unresolved, the existence of serious environmental problems is always detectable. The aspects are closely related. We have all read about scientific studies or even seen television broadcasts that talk about the relationship between Covid-19 and the environment. Many scholars and scientists tell us that the pandemic is linked not only to the issue of the virus passing from animals to humans, but also to environmental factors and pollution. The National Recovery and Resilience Plan can and must be used to address the problems that policy has not wanted to face in recent years, all too often turning its face on the other side. We are all faced with an immense job to do; we must prepare to make Italy a stronger country, ready to face even new challenges that could come after that of Covid. The time to think about it is now. Mr President, in conclusion, we will not have another planet where we can stay. This is our place and this is our home: we have to take care of it and now. That it is indeed for Italy a new Renaissance I wish for the environment, for our children, for their future and for all of us. [[Applause.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u104	Mr President, I will take a few minutes and apologise if I have to go to the House, where a sitting begins at 4 p.m. and the resolution on a report will also be voted on. We must work on two levels - I say this to Parliament - because on 21 July we made an agreement for a seven-year budget, which is very important and it is not true that it lowers resources. As Italy, for the first time in our history, we will find ourselves at final, a net taxpayer country, but beneficiary of resources on agriculture, defence, border management and all the projects that concern the budget, together with this 750 billion fund that we wanted and built after a long political battle. This is the work we will be doing in the coming weeks and months and I believe that involving us all is not an invitation to good feelings, but a necessity for those who believe in the national interest and want to change this country in a greener, fairer and even faster way. We need to build a Plan that has growth as the horizon, understood in the fundamental values of this country: an impact on gender equity, as Senator Bonino and Senator Fedeli have said; an impact on inequalities, reading all European statistics and rankings in which Italy, from kindergartens to female employment, to school dispersal, is still an unfair country. If this work, which is planning, on the basis of interests that are national, will be done - together - by the Parliament, the Government, the Regions, the Municipalities and the social actors, then it will not be the victory of a government or a majority, but it will be the idea of a country that we all have to share. ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u106	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u108	STEGER (Aut (SVP-PATT, UV)). Mr President, I should like to begin with a few methodological considerations. I agree with what Mr Gualtieri said about the considerations of departure. No to projects that produce fragmentation, that are unable to generate economies of scale, that are difficult to monitor and evaluate and that already in the past have shown serious critical design. I also welcome the fact that the path taken by the European Union (the guidelines, the outline plan and the final plan) is being submitted to Parliament from time to time. I am a little less convinced that the Regions and the Autonomous Provinces should only be involved in the implementation phase of the projects. Although the Minister has now said that involvement is a strong will of the Government, greater involvement is not only desirable in matters where local authorities have direct or concurrent competence with the Central State, but for all projects whose effectiveness is closely linked to the work of the nearest body. Local autonomy, therefore, must have a significant voice also in the design phase of the entire plant, because only in this way an effective path is built; all this also with bilateral interlocations between individual Regions, Government and Ministries, as the Province of Bolzano has also urged in the plan that it has sent to the Government with the planning proposals for our territory; all this, also to generate a complementarity with the resources of programming 2021-2027, thus avoiding duplication, overlaps or, worse still, waste of resources. Having said that, to write a plan that looks at the next 20 years, we need to understand what has happened in the last 20 years. The main reason for low Italian growth is the stagnation of productivity, which here has grown by 3%, compared to 25 in Germany or 30-32 in the United States. Productivity remained low because Italy did not make full use of the IT revolution. To give an idea, in agriculture only 4% of companies have been able to benefit from technological change. At the same time, there is not a single Italian company among the great world players in the sector, when, in the meantime, Chinese companies and others, with state subsidies, try to create a real monopoly of supply of new generation networks. The Plan's guidelines adequately address this challenge. In the final plan, however, we must also give proper attention to the small ones; that is, we must prevent this process from investing only the larger companies, the more modern ones, and the better public administrations. We need to think about the small business owner, the disadvantaged territories or the family business, which is not always prone to change; therefore broadband for rural areas, connection to the network for agricultural enterprises and a specific plan for digital development in mountain areas. Unfortunately, the word mountain lacks in its policy lines, objectives and challenges and is only found indirectly when it comes to environmental protection. Yet, in the mountains we play a fundamental game, that of safeguarding biodiversity. In the mountains there are the best examples of environmentally sustainable enterprises; in the mountains the fight against hydrogeological failure means the fight against depopulation, because less people means even less care of the woods, fields and land and more stress and more problems for metropolitan centres and urban areas. To return to the policy lines, the commitment to the first two long-term objectives is welcomed: doubling growth, with the minimum objective of bringing it at least in line with the European one of 1.6% of the last decade, and achieving an increase in the employment rate by at least 10 points. These two objectives suggest the priority reforms that will have to accompany the plan, according to the path outlined by the Government; they are of course the reform of the tax and that of the job. The question of taxation, as I have already said in this House, is not so much about the model as about its clarity: to know what is paid and why, perhaps also knowing that everyone pays, because tax evasion, already unbearable before the crisis, now takes on the clothes of a real abuse. On this point we must not be afraid: those who are in difficulty must have the hand of a new social pact, that of a new beginning, in which, on the one hand, we offer a path of sustainable return, but, on the other, we do not give up protecting those who, on the other hand, pay taxes all of them and always. This is a form of respect for citizens who, while being able to suspend their payments, have not done so in order not to put the state's coffers in difficulty. On the job, the first point is to put hand to the Dignity Decree and re-introduce vouchers or other tools for greater flexibility on the recruitment front, as well as need a deep rethinking of citizenship income as an active policy for work, which has completely failed in this objective. I say this to the friends of the majority: it is not the time to defend old flags, but to imagine new roads. I also say this to the friends who represent the business world: opposing support for businesses and workers makes no sense, because the integration fund, for example, boosts demand and therefore supports the same companies. Before concluding, Mr President, I would like to make two final reflections on the long-term objectives. Ecological conversion is at the heart of the European project and the figure on which success will be measured in individual countries. Those who spend their resources properly will have a more modern and competitive production system. If a country does not do so or does it harm, its business system will pay a very high price in terms of competitiveness. The higher part will be paid by the new generations, because you will not be able to bring back the debt that is now created. This is why priority must be given to me, which is called renewable sources and hydrogen, in order to exploit the potential of the mountain, but above all to heal the environmental wounds of the great industry, which today has to change its forms of energy supply. The second reflection concerns the fact that I would have liked to read, among the priorities, the commitment to unlocking the social elevator. This notion has disappeared from the public debate of the country, but it is fundamental for any society that sets itself the goal of social and economic growth: the idea that with my commitment and my skill I can have better living conditions than my parents. Too bad there isn't; it seems to me a big limit. I should like to conclude, Mr President, by saying that the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and European Democrats welcomes these policy lines. As we have said, our hope is that local self-government will be more involved in the construction of the draft plan and the final plan, but also that the gaps I have expressed so far will be filled. However, we recognize the Government's efforts, the goodness of the Commission's discussions and an overall framework of objectives, missions and interventions, which we agree with. [[Applause.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u109	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u110	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u111	GINETTI (IV-PSI). Mr President, today we begin in the Senate an important path of confrontation for the definition of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan, starting from the guidelines, awaiting the European Regulation and in view of the coming April deadline, because we are aware of the importance of the match for our country and for the whole of Europe. Without a doubt, the agreement reached at the July European Council on Next generation EU and recovery fund represents a milestone in the process of European integration, with the sharing of a debt, finally common and European, of 750 billion out of a total of resources of more than 1,074 billion. It is an opportunity for Italy to overcome the serious crisis generated by the pandemic, but also to face the many nodes that in the past have curbed the processes of innovation and reform and thus accelerate the modernization, support the growth potential and thus create the conditions for a real structural transformation of the country. With EUR 208 billion, no doubt, Italy is one of the biggest beneficiaries of Next Generation EU funds and it is precisely on their use that we are playing our credibility to overcome the infrastructural and employment gaps of population growth, but also of education and training compared to the European average. It is a crisis that risks being paid in particular by young people and women - as has been mentioned - to whom we must therefore pay particular attention, because the rebirth of the country must go precisely from women's work and youth employment. We therefore agree with the approach of the guidelines and the challenges set out in the Plan as part of the six missions in which the document is based. We are moving towards the green transition and the climate neutrality of the green new deal by 2050, to which at least 37% of the resources are allocated; 20% are going to the digital transition; then there is territorial and social cohesion, but also addressing the structural reforms indicated in the European Semester: reform of civil and criminal justice towards shorter processes. Then there is public administration reform to make it more efficient and accessible; tax reform to make it simpler, fairer and more transparent; labour reform, but also education and skills development of the next generation to which we are leaving an important debt. We believe that these reforms are necessary in order to convert the public system and to achieve the objective of doubling the average rate of growth of the national economy, which means more public investment, but also stimulating private investment. We aim - for example - to support the technological transition of companies, strengthening Enterprise 4.0, launched by our Government already last legislature, towards lasting and structural development and growth and therefore able to guarantee the future financial sustainability of public debt; sustainability only postponed with the suspension of the rules of the Stability Pact. In this context, the resources of the National Plan must be considered, Minister, additional and complementary, and not substitute for the ordinary resources of the State: I am thinking - for example - of the 80 billion of cohesion. It is a great opportunity - as has been said - that we cannot waste. Then there are three tasks that await us: defining the strategic horizon; identifying the measures and projects to be sung with coherent, measurable and measurable objectives at every stage, but also efficient management organisation, in order to ensure that the time of commitment of expenditure between 2023 and 2026; sharing of the Plan especially with those actuators called in co-responsibility of expenditure, between Regions and Municipalities. It is not a simple and non-trivial operation for a country like ours which has always been in difficulty on the full use of European resources and, in particular, the Structural Funds. That is why we share the need to set up a specific task force for the governance of the Plan, which also serves as a direction and guarantee, with more streamlined management procedures. However - this is important to us - we must now seize the opportunity of the crisis as a push for the necessary change, in order to make the ordinary activity of public administration and the entire country system more efficient. In other words, we must have the courage to act with reforms precisely on those bottlenecks that have condemned the Italian economy to a succession of stages of stagnation over the last twenty years. Therefore, it is not enough to pull out projects from the drawers - we agree - but be careful not to exclude from the Plan those investments that, although dated over time, maintain feasibility and are still to be considered strategic, in order to recover the gaps of infrastructural and territorial development. I am thinking of the bridge over the Strait of Messina, but also of the many high-speed railway projects, in particular for the revival of the South, from which the relaunch of the entire country necessarily passes. But above all, let us not forget the cities and their suburbs: we could call it the aesthetics of green transformation or new Renaissance. For example, let's resume the reminder plan, which we have proposed since 2015. Making our suburbs more beautiful, more inclusive and safer means upgrading public and private buildings, from popular homes to hospitals; making them more energy efficient, greener and more livable, from the point of view of urban mobility, more alive and productive and capable of creating jobs, but also education, with more beautiful and safer schools. It is therefore necessary to rethink the places of life, from the cultural landscape of our immense historical-architectonic heritage, to the natural and urban landscape, but above all it is necessary to put in safety against the hydrogeological risk, as evidenced by the devastations of last week, and for this reason we felt urgent to ask for the restoration of the unity of mission Italy safe. It is also necessary to clean up polluted sites and abandoned areas and to maintain the many bridges and viaducts still under observation today. Not least, but first of all, there is health: territorial health for health protection, but also as a safeguard for development, as we have well understood by the effects of the pandemic and the resulting lockdown. For this reason, and in parallel, we cannot afford not to activate the EUR 36 billion, available immediately with the ESM, all the more so now that we have asked to extend the state of emergency until January. They are funds available immediately and at rates close to zero. So we face an unprecedented challenge, with the definition of the National Recovery and Resilience Plan, to talk to the future and make the Italy system competitive and attractive, with resources that we are borrowing only from our children. To conclude, the exceptional nature of the pandemic has undoubtedly increased the awareness that the European Union must rely more on its own internal engine of growth. This was why a Europeanist government was needed, convinced that the country's interest is also being played in Europe and not against Europe. Mr President, transition means transition and someone has urged us not only to go through change. It is time to design it and the National Plan for the Commitment of Next Generation EU Resources can be its tool. We will help the Government and, for this reason, I declare the Italian Viva-PSI Group's vote in favour of the motion for a majority resolution. [[Applause.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2020-10-13-LEG18-Senato-sed-264.ana.u114	FAZZOLARI (FdI). Mr President, I have seen - and much to be appreciated - that the Deputy Minister took notes on his smartphone as Senator Nadia Ginetti spoke. This is very nice. Evidently the notes he took will serve. Maybe he wants to do the same while I speak; do as he pleases, but he can also listen to me and the notes will have them from the official Senate text. Let's start the complex process of Next generation EU and recovery fund. Unfortunately, the truth operation begins. We have heard a bit about everything in this period; now the actual phase begins, we start from the moment zero with the guidelines, then the plans will arrive, but it is the moment when all the fantasies and the chatter made fade away, and it is time to do an operation truth. The famous movie with Alberto Sordi comes to mind, "An American in Rome": "You are now twenty-one years old, it is time for you to know whose son you are." Someone has to tell you the truth. Let's try it ourselves. We have heard that 209 billion would have come because in Europe someone loves us and, because of the great consideration they have of our Government in Europe, more money would have come to Italy than to the rest of Europe. Now, whoever took the trouble to read the documents will surely know that the resources were allocated on the basis of objective criteria: GDP per capita compared to the average GDP of the European Union; average unemployment over the last five years - from 2020 to 2022 - and then, from 2023, per capita income and projected decline in GDP. Italy excels in these three parameters: GDP per capita lower than the European average, because, if this House has escaped, the rich Italy, from the European budget, has an average of GDP per capita lower than the European average, a great boast that can be made. According to great pride it is a rate of unemployment in the last five years higher than the European average, because for years we have been behind the European trend. Thirdly, the projected fall in GDP for Italy is greater than for other countries. Result: we take more than the others. And there was someone bragging. Part of it is the logic of citizenship income: people who brag about taking state assistance money while some idiot, on the other hand, works. So, let's say that there was not much to boast about being among the dumbest in Europe and, therefore, to take more resources, thanks to the policies of the last ten years of the PD. If someone takes the trouble to read European documents instead of always saying imaginative things, he will find that recently the ECB has published a document in which it relates the aid lost in relation to the GDP of individual states, and Italy is the ninth country. We are not first, we are ninth, and this would have been the most important criterion because, as the laws of financing in Italy work, the contribution to lost money relates to turnover, and so the GDP of a nation, and we are not the first of the class, but we are the ninth. The second truth operation: the flood of money that was to come from the European Union. A few government friendly newspapers published on the front page: Prime Minister Count submerged banknotes. Is that so? Here again, the EU budget is 1,000 billion for the next seven years. With those 1,000 billion, Italy tended to end up at less than 30 billion between what it gives and what it receives. With the resources put in place to deal with the crisis, instead, that is, 750 billion total, to Italy they go in theory more than 200, of which 128, however, are loaned. These are resources that we would have taken on the markets anyway and therefore the advantage is not 128 billion, but the difference between the interest rate that we would pay on the markets and the money that we take instead, a calculation that is difficult to do. On the other hand, we have 81 billion grants; we would have finished the seven-year period with less than 30 billion and, therefore, we have more than 50 billion from the European Union. This is important, but this is the order of magnitude; 81 billion grants over four years, from 2020 to 2023, account for about 2% of Italian expenditure over the four years. It's a help, but that's the order of magnitude. They told us that the resources would arrive immediately and we know that they will not arrive before June 2021. This is the painting. We then have government guidelines. These lines are general: let's not argue about them and we said that we are zero year. However, we asked for four fundamental priorities to be included in the guidelines: the post-earthquake reconstruction and the contrast to the hydrogeological failure, which was not included in the priority measures of this Government and we regret it, although thanks to the Brothers of Italy these voices at least appear in the document. We have also asked for security to be included, because anyone dealing with the labour market and production knows that no one invests in an unsafe environment. Spending European resources to increase the security in which our economic operators operate is an advantage for GDP; with those resources you can also finally make the prison plan that has not always been implemented in Italy for lack of resources. We regret that this too was not included in the measures, although it was accepted into the body of the text. There is also support for the birth rate; this term is mentioned only once throughout the text very quickly, when we know that it is one of Italy's major economic problems. We then asked to devote those resources to an industrial policy finally converting everything that is not identifiable as an Italy brand into what is identifiable as such. We cannot believe in thirty years of being competitive in steel production - to give an example among many - because there will be no one willing to pay for Italian steel more than Indian or Chinese steel. In the world, however, many realities are willing to pay more a product branded in Italy because it is part of the shipbuilding of the car, of the avant-garde industries; we had proposed, therefore, to use this process for a gradual conversion of our productive fabric. We regret that this too has not been accepted. Since we are trying to do a real operation, we must tell ourselves that unfortunately the Government has demonstrated all its inability in recent months. It is not an opinion and I quote only data. In the ten decrees issued in the period of emergency Covid, there are 181 implementing decrees. There are 14 implementing decrees out of 34; 94 out of 137 of the decree are missing; 8 out of 8 are missing of the decree liquidity, having been issued zero; 20 out of 20 of the decree simplifications are missing, because even in this case they have been issued zero; in the Note of update to the Document of economy and finance, just published, there is a lower debt of 13 billion not for some virtuosity, but for lack of draw of the measures put in field by the Government and, therefore, further failure. We are therefore genuinely concerned about how the Government will manage the resources available. We think that the most useful thing the government can do for the good of the nation is to leave the field to someone who might be more capable and, above all, give the voice back to the Italians so that they can decide which Government and with what political and economic vision to restart the nation after the serious crisis in which we live. [[Applause.]]

I expect this awareness to be translated into the budget into a strategic choice of assuming these powers immediately, to be allocated to the central administration and decentralised administrations (regions and municipalities) to work together. Finally, although I would have had other things to say, the third big issue is the need for transversality, which I am giving some examples of. The environment is not simply a policy, but a strategic choice that cuts across all policies; gender policies are not a policy, they cut across all policies; policies for the new generations cut across all policies. The ability not to reason for watertight compartments is decisive; it is not a question of putting an extra word or an extra chapter. As far as industrial policies are concerned, without ecological transition, without green transition, I do not understand what industrial policies we can do. This is the leap in quality, and on this we must move from words to deeds. I expect that in the next phase of the projects there will be the sign of this radical innovation; otherwise, when we go to talk to the associations that will ask us (and not rarely ask us) the same things, we must challenge them forward, not chase the positions that they propose us. I also say this to the opposition, because the challenge is not consent now, but trying to really change this country now. Otherwise we would make a very mediocre and petty choice [[Applause.]]
